# Narwhal 사건 기록 (Lessons Log)

> `CLAUDE.md`의 Mistakes Log를 그대로 옮긴 아카이브. 매 턴 재전송되는 컨텍스트 파일에는
> **여러 사건에 걸쳐 일반화되는 규칙만** 남기고(→ `docs/common/agent-operational-rules.md`), 날짜별 사건
> 서술은 여기에 보관한다. 새 사건이 발생하면: 서술은 이 파일에, 일반 규칙이 도출되면 그것만
> `docs/common/agent-operational-rules.md`에 추가.
>
> 항목 추가 형식: `| YYYY-MM-DD | 사건 설명 | 조치 |`

## Mistakes Log (Compounding Engineering)

> Add entries here whenever Claude makes a mistake. The same mistake will not be repeated.
> Rules that generalize across incidents graduate into `docs/common/agent-operational-rules.md`.

| 2026-10-31 | Portal browser E2E가 간헐적으로 멈췄다(#462). 모드 1(10-23): harden-runner v2.22.1 `egress-policy: block`에서 허용 목록의 `github.com:443` checkout이 2ms 만에 거부. 모드 2(10-27, run 40440188219·40538827725): 모든 step 성공 뒤 `Complete job: Cleaning up orphan processes`에서 job timeout(15분)+취소 유예(5분)까지 정지, gate 실패. 모드 2는 v2.22.1·v2.22.2 둘 다에서 나와 dependabot #482(v2.22.2)만으로는 해결되지 않는다. upstream step-security/harden-runner#708: 본문은 허용 도메인의 A 레코드 중 하나만 기록하고 나머지 IP를 drop하는 증상(westus2)이고, 댓글의 다른 보고자가 "post step 뒤 `Cleaning up orphan processes`에서 job timeout까지 멈춘다(모든 step이 통과한 run 포함)"고 적었다. 원인은 runner의 `90-egress-proxy.conf` drop-in과 agent DNS 설정의 충돌로 분석됐다. 2026-10-27 메인테이너가 v2.22.3에 수정을 냈다고 댓글을 달았고, 이슈는 아직 OPEN이다 | 6개 harden-runner 핀을 v2.22.3(`46b06cea…`, 2026-10-27, "GitHub DNS resolver/outbound proxy 실험으로 허용 연결이 막히던 문제 수정"; 태그 커밋 메시지는 `rc-46 … v2.23.0`이지만 메인테이너가 #708에서 이 태그를 지정했다)으로 올렸다. block 정책과 허용 목록은 그대로 두었다(audit 전환 안 함). D-job-timeout(15분)은 유지한다: 완료 단계 hang은 step timeout으로 막을 수 없고 job timeout만 상한을 준다. 효과는 CI 반복 실행으로만 확인할 수 있다(미확인). 재발 시 실패 VM의 region과 agent 로그의 `ip address dropped`를 먼저 본다. 판별자: **'모든 step 성공 + Complete job 정지'는 앱 프로세스보다 egress 에이전트의 DNS/IP 불일치를 먼저 의심한다. 같은 증상이 두 버전에서 나오면 버전 bump PR은 해결 증거가 아니다.** |
| 2026-10-28 | Falco 0.45.1을 Kakao에 올리고 20시간 뒤 라이브 Loki에 이벤트 47,180건이 쌓였고, 그중 47,178건(2건 빼고 전부)이 번들 룰 `Contact K8S API Server From Container`(Notice)였다. 전부 API 서버를 부르는 게 일인 플랫폼 컴포넌트였다. (k8s.ns.name, container.image.repository)별 건수: storage `docker.io/otwld/velero-ui` 43013, devtools `ghcr.io/dasomel/narwhal-portal` 2084, iam `quay.io/keycloak/keycloak-operator` 1807, platform-system `reg.kyverno.io/kyverno/kyverno` 360, devtools `ghcr.io/headlamp-k8s/headlamp` 8, devtools `docker.io/kubernetesui/dashboard-auth` 1, devtools `docker.io/kubernetesui/dashboard-web` 1, platform-system `ghcr.io/cloudnative-pg/cloudnative-pg` 1(총계와 쌍별 건수는 따로 조회한 값이라 정확히 맞지 않고, 수치는 결정과 함께 전달받은 것이며 이 변경이 다시 조회하지 않았다). 번들의 예외 훅 `user_known_contact_k8s_api_server_activities`는 `(never_true)`라 아무것도 제외하지 않는다. 지름길 둘은 피했다: `falco.priority`를 올리면 이 룰만이 아니라 모든 Notice 룰이 사라지고, 네임스페이스·이미지 전체 와일드카드는 탈취된 파드까지 가린다. 잘못된 예외 파일은 조용히 무시되지 않는다: 룰 이름 대소문자가 다르면 Falco가 로드 오류로 종료(exit 1)해 모든 노드에서 기동이 멈춘다(0.45.1에서 확인, 없는 필드와 짧은 튜플도 `falco -V`에서 같은 Invalid). | `falco.yaml`의 `customRules`(차트가 ConfigMap `falco-rules`로 만들어 `/etc/falco/rules.d`에 마운트하고 `falco.rules_files`가 번들 룰 다음에 로드)에 예외를 **추가(append)**했다(D-FALCO-NOISE): `override: {exceptions: append}`, `fields: [k8s.ns.name, container.image.repository]`, `comps: [=, =]`, 위 8개 정확한 쌍만. 실제 falco:0.45.1 이미지에서 `falco -V`로 번들 룰과 이 파일이 통과하고, 룰 이름 대소문자 오류('no rule by that name already exists')·`override` 없는 재정의('Item has no mapping for key enabled')·없는 필드·짧은 튜플은 거부됨을 확인했다. 랩(Falco 0.45.1, 렌더된 설정, kubelet 방식 심볼릭 링크 rules.d)에서 레지스트리가 보이는 5개 쌍(ghcr.io·quay.io·reg.kyverno.io)은 이벤트가 없고, 네임스페이스만 다르거나 이미지만 다르거나 두 쌍을 섞거나 k8s 라벨이 없는 파드는 이벤트 1건씩 남았다(예외 파일 없이는 10개 모두 1건). docker.io 쌍 3개는 랩의 docker 엔진이 `docker.io/` 접두어를 떼서(`otwld/velero-ui`) 문법만 검증했고, 라이브 CRI는 접두어를 유지한다. R291 A11이 append·룰 이름·필드·comps·정확한 8쌍(glob·빈 값 없음)·단일 항목·`falco.priority: notice`·`rules.d` 로드 순서를 변이 19개로 고정하고, Falco 이미지가 바뀌면 룰 이름·필드를 `falco -V`로 다시 확인할 때까지 실패한다. 비용: 그 8개 워크로드의 API 연결은 Falco가 더 이상 표시하지 않는다(kube-apiserver 감사 정책은 쓰기와 secrets·configmaps·serviceaccounts·RBAC 읽기만 남기고 그 밖의 get/list/watch는 버린다). 새 플랫폼 클라이언트는 이 룰의 Notice로 먼저 보이므로 확인 뒤 정확한 쌍을 `falco.yaml`과 R291 `NOISE_PAIRS`에 추가한다. 배포 후 확인: `kubectl -n security-system get cm falco-rules`가 있고 DaemonSet이 롤링 뒤 노드 수만큼 Ready이며 Falco 기동 로그의 룰 목록에 `/etc/falco/rules.d/narwhal-api-noise.yaml`이 `schema validation: ok`로 보여야 하고, Loki의 이 룰 건수는 8쌍에서 0으로 떨어지고 그 밖의 네임스페이스·이미지 파드의 API 호출은 여전히 한 건씩 남아야 한다. |
| 2026-10-28 | Falco를 0.39.2(차트 4.16.0)에서 0.45.1(차트 9.2.1)로 올려 전 프로바이더에서 다시 켰다(D-FALCO-045). 마이그레이션에서 드러난 침묵 실패 여덟 가지. (1) 구 설정의 Loki `extralabels: "source,rule,priority,hostname"`와 주석 "Loki 라벨 source=falco"는 틀렸다. falcosidekick 2.31~2.35 `outputs/loki.go`는 `source`를 Falco 이벤트 소스(`syscall`)로 쓰고, 리터럴 `source=falco`는 Alertmanager 알림(`outputs/alertmanager.go`에 하드코딩)에만 있다. (2) 기본 `loki.format: text`는 출력 문자열만 저장하므로 모든 줄을 `JSON.parse`하는 포털(`falco.ts`)이 전부 조용히 버린다. (3) falcosidekick이 쓰는 `priority` 라벨은 `Critical`처럼 대문자로 시작하는데(실캡처와 업스트림 `loki_test.go`가 일치) 포털은 소문자 `priority="critical"`로 조회하므로 우선순위 필터와 `getCriticalRuntimeAlerts`는 일치하는 스트림이 없다. (4) 차트 9.x는 `falco.grpc`/`falco.grpc_output`가 있으면 렌더에 실패(`removedConfigGuard`)하지만, 7.0.0에서 제거된 `collectors.containerd`는 조용히 무시되고 기본 엔진 6개의 hostPath(`/var/run`, `/run/podman`, `/run/crio` 등)가 privileged 파드에 마운트된다. (5) 차트 기본 falcoctl(init+sidecar)은 `ghcr.io/falcosecurity`에서 룰과 container 플러그인을 런타임에 받는데 Kakao 노드는 ghcr.io에 닿지 못한다. 플러그인(`libcontainer.so` 0.7.5)과 룰(falco-rules 5.2.0)은 이미지에 내장되어 있다. (6) `metrics.outputRule: true`면 `priority: notice`여도 "Falco internal: metrics snapshot"(Informational)이 시작 시와 매 interval마다 Loki로 가 포털 피드(`{rule=~".+"}`)에 노드마다 시간당 한 줄씩 섞인다. (7) falcosidekick 서브차트 0.14.1은 `Chart.yaml` appVersion이 2.31.1인데 실제 이미지는 `values.yaml`의 2.32.0이고, 2.32.0은 취약한 Go 모듈(falcosecurity/falcosidekick#1366, 2.34.0에서 수정)과 Loki+Alertmanager를 함께 구성할 때의 데이터 레이스(falcosecurity/falcosidekick#1390, 2.35.0에서 수정)를 고치기 전 버전이다. trivy(2026-10-28, CRITICAL/HIGH)는 2.32.0이 9/80, 2.35.0이 3/19, 2.35.1이 0/1이다. 2.35.0은 쿨링 7일을 넘긴 최신 릴리스(2026-08-31)이고 2.35.1(2026-10-26)은 2026-11-02부터 후보다(D-FALCO-SIDEKICK). 차트의 helm test 훅 파드(`curlimages/curl`, 태그 없음)는 narwhal values로 렌더할 때만 생기고(falcosidekick은 서브차트 기본 비활성) gitops-engine 소스상 ArgoCD가 훅 타입을 몰라 무시한다. `01-generate-image-list.sh --live`는 번들 차트를 기본 values로 렌더하므로 이 파드를 목록에 끌어오지 않는다(실측: 기본 values 렌더는 훅 이미지가 비어 있고 `--set falcosidekick.enabled=true`일 때만 `curlimages/curl`). 구 설정의 `loki.tenantmap`·`alertmanager.expirealert`는 차트가 모르는 키였다. (8) 차트 0.14.1은 `ALERTMANAGER_ENDPOINT=/api/v1/alerts`를 보내 falcosidekick 자체 기본값을 덮어쓰는데, Alertmanager 0.27+는 v1 API를 제거해 410 Gone을 돌려준다(우리 v0.33.0, 클러스터 핀과 같은 digest로 실측). falcosidekick은 `AlertManager - 410 Gone`만 남기고 Critical 알림을 전부 버린다(실제 Falco Critical 이벤트로 재현, `GET /api/v2/alerts`는 `[]`). `endpoint: /api/v2/alerts`로 바꾸면 `POST OK (200)`이고 알림이 `source=falco`로 나타난다. **커널 7.0은 검증되지 않았다.** 로컬 Docker Desktop VM(커널 7.0.14-linuxkit, aarch64)에서 0.45.1 modern_ebpf는 `Opening 'syscall' source with modern BPF probe` 이후 정상 동작했고 컨테이너 이벤트가 falcosidekick(2.32.0과 2.35.0 모두)을 거쳐 Loki 푸시까지 갔고, 실제 Falco Critical 이벤트가 실제 Alertmanager v0.33.0에도 위처럼 전달됐다. 같은 VM에서 0.39.2는 7월과 동일하게 `An error occurred in an event source` → `Initialization issues during scap_init`로 종료했다(privileged에서도 실패하므로 7월의 `leastPrivileged` 원인 추정은 틀렸다). 그러나 Ubuntu 26.04 `7.0.0-NN-generic` Vagrant 노드, K8s CRI 경로(`k8s.*` 필드), 수 시간 부하는 관측하지 못했다. | `falco.yaml` 재작성(이미지 태그 고정, falcosidekick 2.35.0, `alertmanager.endpoint: /api/v2/alerts`, falcoctl 비활성, `collectors.containerEngine`은 CRI 엔진만, `loki.format: json`, `outputRule: false`, ServiceMonitor에 `release: prometheus-stack`), 포털 `falco.ts`가 priority를 `Critical`과 `critical` 둘 다 매칭하고 JSON null 필드를 정규화(실캡처를 고정한 vitest 7건)하고 보안 페이지의 Ubuntu 26.04 런타임 탭 숨김(`detectUbuntu2604`)과 i18n 키를 없애고 KISA 통제 문구를 갱신, `14-gitops-bootstrap.sh`의 Ubuntu 26.04 제외 삭제, 에어갭 이미지 2개와 digest·라이선스·차트 소스 등록, R291 `check-falco.py`가 렌더·핀·digest·modern_ebpf·런타임 pull 부재·CRI 전용 엔진·Loki/Alertmanager 계약·포털 접점·Kyverno 제외를 변이 50개로 고정. 판별자: 7월 증상은 `Opening 'syscall' source with modern BPF probe` 바로 뒤의 `scap_init` 종료이고, 0.44.x가 수 분마다 재시작하는 증상(`could not parse param ... expected length`)은 다른 원인(auxmap 경쟁, falcosecurity/libs#3086, 0.45.0에서 수정)이다. 배포 후 확인: `kubectl -n security-system get ds falco`가 노드 수만큼 Ready이고 로그에 `scap_init` 오류가 없어야 하며, Critical 이벤트가 Alertmanager `GET /api/v2/alerts`에 `source=falco`로 보여야 하고, 테스트 파드에서 `cat /etc/shadow` 후 Loki에 `{rule="Read sensitive file untrusted"}` 스트림이 생기고 포털 `/api/security/runtime-events`가 그 이벤트를 돌려줘야 한다. 에어갭 번들에는 차트 타르볼과 이미지 둘(`docker.io/falcosecurity/falco`, `falcosidekick`)을 미러에 올려야 한다. |
| 2026-10-27 | Kakao에서 Kubernetes Dashboard SSO 로그인이 여전히 `401 MSG_LOGIN_UNAUTHORIZED_ERROR`로 실패했다. #478(bastion dnsmasq 복구)의 수정은 요청이 `master-1`(172.17.0.10)에 닿을 때만 성립했다. 클러스터 내부 `kubernetes` Service는 마스터 3대(.10/.11/.12) 전부를 엔드포인트로 두고 있는데, `master-1`만 2026-08-26 `11-4` 실행 이래 `--oidc-*` 플래그 7개와 `/etc/kubernetes/pki/oidc-ca.crt`를 갖고 있었고 `master-2`(.11)·`master-3`(.12)에는 플래그와 CA 파일이 전혀 없었다(초기 설치 매니페스트 그대로). 그래서 Dashboard `/api/v1/login`·Headlamp·kubectl OIDC 토큰이 `master-2/3`에 도달할 때마다(확률 ~2/3) 어느 인증기도 토큰을 인수하지 않아 apiserver 로그에 OIDC 에러조차 없이 `"Unable to authenticate the request" err="invalid bearer token"`으로 거부되었다. 원인: `11-4-keycloak-apiserver.sh`가 `sshpass -p vagrant`로 다른 마스터 전파를 시도하고 실패를 `2>/dev/null \|\| echo "WARN: Could not apply OIDC..."`로 삼켰는데, 클라우드 노드에는 `vagrant` 비밀번호도 노드 간 SSH 신뢰도 없어(`distribute-join-kakao.sh`처럼 항상 host→bastion→node로만 이동) 매번 WARN으로만 끝나고 넘어갔다. 게다가 `test-sso.sh`와 `verify-cluster.sh`는 로컬 매니페스트 또는 kube-apiserver 파드의 `.items[0]`만 검사해 모든 검증이 PASS였다. | D-APISERVER-OIDC-ALL: (1) 노드 로컬 스크립트 `scripts/cluster/11-4b-apiserver-oidc-node.sh` 하나로 7개 `--oidc-*` 플래그 작성, CA 비어 있음/PEM/`openssl x509` 검증, `/etc/kubernetes/kube-apiserver.yaml.pre-oidc-<UTC>` 외부 백업(첫 적용과 CA/annotation 갱신 모두 변경 전에 쓰고 이전 백업을 덮어쓰지 않는다. `manifests/` 내부 백업은 kubelet이 정적 파드를 `namespace/name`으로 구분하므로 같은 이름의 사본이 실제 매니페스트와 충돌하거나 가려서 금지), dotfile 스테이징 후 원자적 `mv`, 재시작 확인(변경 직전 kube-apiserver PID를 기록해 PID가 바뀐 뒤에만 로컬 `/readyz`를 폴링한다. kubelet이 변경을 감지해 멈출 때까지 옛 프로세스가 `ok`로 답해 `/readyz`만 보면 아직 재시작하지 않은 마스터를 두고 다음 마스터를 재시작해 apiserver 2대가 동시에 내려갈 수 있었다. PID를 못 읽으면 고정 sleep 뒤 `/readyz`로 되돌아가는 best effort), 그리고 CA 재적용을 일원화했다. 매니페스트 annotation `narwhal.dasomel.io/oidc-ca-sha256`이 설치된 CA와 다르면 그 값을 갱신해 kubelet이 정적 파드를 다시 만들게 한다(`touch`는 파드 해시를 바꾸지 않고, Kakao sudo PATH에 `crictl`이 없음). 매니페스트를 디렉터리 밖으로 빼지 않으므로 중간에 죽어도 apiserver가 내려간 채로 남지 않는다. (2) `11-4`는 `master-1`과 Vagrant의 `master-2/3`에 이 스크립트를 호출하며 어느 마스터에서든 실패 시 즉시 비-0 종료하고, `PROVIDER=kakao`에서는 노드 간 SSH를 시도하지 않고 운영자 명령을 안내한다. Vagrant의 `sshpass` 호출에는 `-o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null`을 더했다(재생성된 마스터의 낡은 known_hosts 항목이 있으면 OpenSSH가 비밀번호 인증을 꺼서 Phase 2가 멈춘다). 발급자 엔드포인트에 닿지 못하면 이전에는 경고 후 건너뛰었지만 이제 exit 1로 Phase 2가 멈춘다(11-4는 `CRITICAL_SCRIPTS` 항목이며, 비-critical인 08-6/10-dnsmasq가 경고만 냈거나 bastion DNS가 내려간 경우에 발생, #478). (3) `scripts/cloud/apply-apiserver-oidc-kakao.sh`가 host→bastion→node 경로로 `master-1`의 CA를 읽어 `master-2..N`에 순차 적용하고(첫 재시작 전에 모든 마스터에 노드 스크립트가 있는지 확인해 없으면 `stage-kakao-nodes.sh` 재실행을 안내하고, CA는 노드에서 `mktemp`한 고유 경로로 올린다) 미러 파드 수가 마스터 수와 같은지와 모든 `kube-apiserver` 미러 파드의 `--oidc-*` 플래그 개수를 검증하며, `provision-kakao.sh`의 `stage_phase2` 말미와 단독 `oidc` 스테이지에 연결했다. (4) `verify-cluster.sh`·`test-sso.sh`는 모든 `kube-apiserver` 파드를 순회해 7개 플래그 누락 파드를 이름으로 FAIL 처리하고, 파드 수가 `control-plane` 라벨 노드 수와 다르면(미러 파드가 없는 마스터) FAIL 처리한다. 회귀 방지: R290(정적 계약 및 변이 30개)과 R290b(행동 하네스 11케이스: PID가 바뀌지 않으면 두 재시작 경로 모두 실패, 지연된 PID 변경은 기다린 뒤에만 `/readyz`, CA/annotation 갱신 경로 백업 포함). 라이브 Kakao는 오케스트레이터가 `master-2/3`에 동일 CA·플래그를 원자적 `mv`로 반영한 뒤 위조 JWT 프로브로 세 마스터 모두에서 `oidc: verify token: failed to verify signature`(OIDC 인증기 도달)를 확인했다(사용자 브라우저 로그인 검증은 별도). 후속 과제: `kubeadm-config` ClusterConfiguration에는 이 플래그가 없으므로 향후 `kubeadm upgrade` 시 매니페스트 재생성 대응이 필요하다. 판별자: **다중 마스터 뒤의 Service(`kubernetes`)에서 간헐적 401이 나면 `.items[0]`이나 `master-1` 하나만 보지 말고 모든 `kube-apiserver` 파드의 커맨드라인과 로그를 전수 대조한다.** |

R287이 존재를 검사하고 삭제 변이를 잡는다(변이 13개). 판별자: **설정 키를 지울 때는 이름이 가리키는 기능만 보지 말고, 그 키를 읽는 모든 코드 경로를 소스에서 찾는다. `REVERSE_PROXY_*`라는 이름이어도 인증과 무관한 클라이언트 IP 처리가 같은 키를 쓴다.** |

(2) `scripts/common/lib.sh`의 `gitea_upsert_keycloak_source`가 `gitea admin auth list`에서 `Name==keycloak` 행의 ID를 찾아 있으면 `update-oauth --id <id>`(`runUpdateOauth`가 플래그 누락 시 `TwoFactorPolicy`를 지우므로 매번 `--skip-local-2fa` 전달), 없으면 `add-oauth --name keycloak`을 stderr를 버리지 않고 실행한다. 팀 매핑은 `--group-team-map-removal`로 양방향이라 developer→viewer로 바뀐 사용자는 다음 로그인 때 Developers에서 빠진다(독립 리뷰에서 누락 지적, R287b, 변이 5개). | 2026-10-27 | `scripts/cluster/06-phase2-start.sh`의 `CRITICAL_SCRIPTS`에 `11-3-keycloak-clients.sh`가 포함되지 않아, PR #478에서 클라이언트 시크릿 읽기 실패 시 `return 1`로 fail closed 되도록 강화되었음에도 Phase 2에서는 비-critical 스크립트로 취급되어 WARN만 남기고 계속 진행되었다(#491). 이로 인해 11-3 실패 시 ArgoCD, Grafana, Gitea, Harbor, OpenBao 등의 OIDC 시크릿이 비어 있거나 낡은 상태로 남아 12-gitea, 13-argocd 및 14 단계로 전파되어 사후 SSO 401 장애로 이어질 수 있었다. | 11-3 내부 명령을 분석하여 클린 설치(FRESH install) 시 아직 존재하지 않는 리소스(Gitea 파드, ArgoCD ConfigMap, Harbor API, Headlamp deployment, OpenBao 파드) 관련 경로가 모두 skip/WARN으로 안전하게 가드되어 있음을 확인하고, `06-phase2-start.sh`의 `CRITICAL_SCRIPTS`에 `11-3-keycloak-clients.sh`를 추가했다(11-2 직후, 11-4/12 직전). 회귀 방지를 위해 R289(`check-phase2-critical-11-3.py`: `CRITICAL_SCRIPTS` 항목과 실제 실행 루프에서 12-gitea보다 앞선 순서, 변이 2개)를 추가했다. 판별자: **fail-closed로 동작을 바꾼 부속 스크립트는 이를 호출하는 상위 오케스트레이터의 critical script 목록에도 반영되어야 장애가 무시되지 않는다.** |

Near-match: the preceding 2026-10-15 newline row, which fixed the byte parity of the value but not the decision to generate it. | Existence is now probed separately (`-o name`); an existing Secret without a valid 64-hex password aborts instead of regenerating, and first creation uses plain `kubectl create` (apply only on later runs) with an AlreadyExists loser path that re-reads the winner's password. Discriminator: **an empty jsonpath result conflates "absent" and "present but invalid"; probe existence first and treat only a confirmed absence as permission to generate.** Not verified against a real API server. |

Live verification on a cluster is still pending. |

### Agent Instruction Mistakes

| Date | Mistake | Fix |
|------|---------|-----|
| 2026-10-14 | PR320/321 Markdown gates failed after lesson rows were moved during rebase, leaving consecutive blank lines; executable checks passed but the docs were not fully verified. | Collapse only the introduced section-boundary blank lines and run both full-doc markdownlint and changed-line checks before pushing. Discriminator: a clean diff-check does not check MD012 blank-line rules. |
| 2026-10-14 | Started local static checks before a concurrent rebase finished; R52 failed on the changing tree while the final head CI and stable rerun passed. | Finish rebases and edits before verification in the same worktree; keep independent work in separate trees. Discriminator: commands with the same name can observe different intermediate files when git is rewriting the worktree. |
| 2026-10-08 | SBOM fix trace passed evaluate.py but PR evidence-quality gate rejected untyped command evidence. | Prefix actually executed test evidence with `test:` and include the trace path in changeContext; run both evaluators locally. Discriminator: behavior score 100% does not validate evidence-quality typing. |
| 2026-09-28 | The SessionStart hook injected the hardware fact “Master 4GB” into every session, and it went stale when `Vagrantfile` was bumped to 6GB. PR #256's audit fixed the same wrong “patterns in CLAUDE.md” attribution in two files but missed a third. | Update the hook to match the `Vagrantfile` comment and correct the remaining attribution (and this log's own preamble, which still pointed general rules at a nonexistent `CLAUDE.md` "Recurring Rules" section). Discriminator: when a source changes, grep for every restatement of facts in instruction files and hooks. |
| 2026-10-05 | (Mistake made while fixing) While recording the openforge#54 replay trace for `narwhal-verification`, the edge-case result was typed as a `verification` event; `.agents/evals/gate.py` then reported a `bug-fix-verification` regression because that check only counts `regression_verification` events (`evaluate.py`). Separately, the first edge-case mutation used by the reviewer (`adminPassword: "x"`) did NOT fail `make security` — the Makefile credential regex ignores values shorter than 4 characters — so "append a literal password" is not a reproducible edge case unless the value is stated. | Retyped the event to `regression_verification` and recorded the exact mutation (`adminPassword: "hunter2hunter2"`, >=4 chars) in the evidence JSON and trace. Discriminator: run `.agents/evals/gate.py --trace ... --baseline ...` right after writing a trace (do not trust that the event type name is free-form), and write the literal mutation into the evidence, never a paraphrase. |

Discriminator: a reported removed artifact that remains referenced must also appear as a missing dependency; cluster/current availability is not offline bundle completeness. |

Discriminator: file existence and matching digest do not prove unique artifact identity in the manifest. |
| 2026-10-08 | Promotion freshness accepted future fetched_at as a negative age and crashed on NaN/infinite SLO or non-object manifest entries (#48). Discriminator: a positive freshness message for a future intake timestamp is not evidence of a current DB. |
| 2026-10-08 | Bundle diff labeled an Apache-2.0 → SSPLv1 license correction unchanged when version/digest stayed equal (#53). Discriminator: immutable bytes do not imply immutable reviewed metadata; diff license policy separately from artifact identity. |
| 2026-10-14 | Upgrade verification accepted UNKNOWN/NOASSERTION/custom licenses while inventory CI rejected every unreviewed identifier (#53); malformed license values caused tracebacks. | Load the inventory policy source for promotion and reject malformed or unreviewed licenses with explicit diagnostics; test actual CLI positive/negative paths. Discriminator: rejecting forbidden licenses alone does not enforce the reviewed-license allowlist. |
| 2026-10-15 | The upgrade bundle verifier (#124) never read the bundle's own generation evidence: `created_at` was checked only by the schema's `format: date-time`, which `jsonschema` skips without a `FormatChecker` and the no-dependency fallback skips entirely, and `provenance` is optional in the schema. A bundle with a year-2099, `yesterday`, or absent `created_at`, or no producer/source, promoted; nothing bounded bundle age. Near-match: the 2026-10-08 security-db freshness row covers `fetched_at` in a different checker, not bundle manifests | `validate_freshness` in `verify-upgrade-bundle.py` rejects absent/non-RFC 3339/future (>5 min skew) `created_at` and blank `provenance.author`/`source_repo`, independent of `jsonschema`; `--max-age-days` (promotion passes `UPGRADE_BUNDLE_MAX_AGE_DAYS`, default 90) rejects stale bundles and `--now` pins the clock so replay gives the same verdict (`test_upgrade_freshness.py`, R82e). Discriminator: a schema `format` keyword is advisory unless the validator is configured to assert it, so test the field with `-S` (no site packages). The age ceiling is opt-in on purpose: enforcing it in CI would turn the committed v1.1.0 manifest red on a calendar date. Still open for #124: referrers, import quarantine, GC safety |
| 2026-10-15 | `09-verify-bundle-completeness.sh` called a bundle's oci/ layout "present" if `oci/<name>/index.json` merely existed (#124): an `index.json` of `{}`, a deleted/truncated blob, or a digest-pinned image (`@sha256:`, saved with `--all --preserve-digests`) whose layout carried different bytes all exited 0, and `manifest.txt` records refs only, so no per-image digest was ever compared. Discriminator: a completeness gate that tests file existence proves the copy ran, not that the bytes are the ones recorded; test it by corrupting a same-size blob. Referrer loss is reported, not failed, or every bundle would fail 09 until fixed. Still open for #124: save-time per-image digest record, load-time pushed-digest compare (needs a registry), referrer copy, layer re-hash |
| 2026-10-15 | Even after 09 hashed layout bytes (#124), `manifest.txt` recorded refs only, so nothing recorded what 02 actually saved: an internally consistent layout swapped in for another image, a different platform, or a stray `oci/<dir>` passed because the layout was only compared with its own `index.json`. Near-match: the row above (layout bytes) lists this as its open "save-time per-image digest record" | 02 now writes `images.digests.json` (image, layout, top-level digest, platform mode, `collapsed_to`, skopeo version; sorted, no timestamp) via `verify-oci-layout.py --inventory-out`, which re-verifies each layout first; 09 passes `--inventory`/`--manifest` and fails closed on an absent/symlinked/duplicate-key/unsorted/unknown-field inventory, an image with no entry, an unrecorded or non-directory `oci/` entry, or a digest/platform mismatch. A bundle saved before this fails 09 on purpose: re-run 02, never synthesize the file from the current disk (that attests whatever is there); 05 does not read it. `test_oci_inventory.py` (R82g) was mutated check by check on a temp copy; the first record claimed 20 of 20 caught, which was wrong: dropping `--manifest` from 09's verifier call survived every test (a manifest-only ghost ref passed) until the gate test for it was added, and the exact mutant list lives in the trace. Review also found that 02 let `docker.io/a/b:c` and `docker.io/a_b:c` share one `oci/` dir and let a shorter re-run leave stale layouts, both only failing later in 09 with a hint that could not help: 02 now fails before touching anything on a layout-name collision (naming both refs) or on `oci/` entries its list does not produce (naming them; nothing is deleted). **The inventory is trust-on-first-save**: anyone who can rewrite both the layout and the inventory passes, it detects accidental corruption and swaps, not a malicious bundle producer, and there is no signature or external pin yet; its `tool` (skopeo version) field is host-dependent, so the file is not byte-identical across hosts. Referrers are still NOT preserved.  Discriminator: a digest check against a record the same tool derives from the same bytes proves nothing, the record must be written at save time. The script had no context guard, so it silently inherited whatever `kubectl config current-context` happened to be, exactly like `show-credentials.sh` did before the PROVIDER fix three days earlier | Guard the runtime modes: `KUBE_CONTEXT` defaults to `narwhal` and a non-matching current context exits 2 with the actual context named, rather than producing plausible failures. `--static` stays context-free so CI is unaffected. Discriminator for the whole class: a verification tool that reads ambient state does not fail on the wrong target, it **passes or fails about the wrong thing** — check what the tool is pointed at before believing either outcome, and prefer tools that name their target and refuse to guess. The tell here was a hostname in the output that belonged to another project |
| 2026-08-09 | `show-credentials.sh` run against Kakao read **every secret correctly and then printed another cluster's node addresses**. `DOMAIN` was already a variable, but the final SSH section hardcoded the Vagrant values (`192.168.56.10-12`, the kube-vip VIP, MetalLB), so on Kakao it confidently reported a VIP that does not exist there and a subnet belonging to nothing. Separately, running it against a halted cluster hung for **five minutes printing nothing**: ~25 secret lookups, each ~15s, because `--request-timeout=3s` still leaves kubectl's API-discovery retries in place | Add the `PROVIDER` branch, but do **not** hardcode node IPs on either side — read them from `kubectl get nodes`, so the next subnet change cannot stale them. Select workers with `-l '!node-role.kubernetes.io/control-plane'`: kubectl's jsonpath has no `!` filter, so `?(!(@.metadata.labels...))` silently yields nothing (the first attempt printed `Workers (unreachable)` against a healthy cluster). Kakao's public IPs come from the tfstate with `BASTION_IP`/`API_LB_IP`/`INGRESS_LB_IP` overrides, because this script also runs on master-1 where no repo checkout exists. Fix the slowness with **one reachability probe up front** (`kubectl get --raw /readyz`), not with per-call timeouts: 5 min → 3 s, and the failure now names the provider-specific cause (tunnel needed vs. VMs halted). Discriminator for the whole class: a script that takes a provider flag but embeds one provider's addresses does not fail on the other provider, it *lies* — grep for literal IPs in any script that has a `PROVIDER` branch |

I had already reported the defect to the user before re-checking | Pin `LC_ALL=C` on `sort`/`uniq`/`comm` whenever the input is non-ASCII — byte comparison is what these checks actually want, and locale collation silently redefines "equal". Discriminator: if a count check says lines are duplicates, print the lines themselves before believing it — a positional `paste <(grep -n …) <(grep -n …)` showed the true sequence immediately and cost nothing. Fifth time in this repo that a clever grep pipeline produced a false alarm, and the first where the false alarm was about a *correct* file rather than a broken one |
| 2026-08-05 | `if kubectl get pod ... \| grep -q "Running" \|\| true; then` — the `\|\| true`, added to dodge pipefail, made the condition **always true**, so the test it looked like it was performing never ran. Found while auditing the 15 remaining `cmd \| grep -q` sites after the chrony bug. Harmless here only by luck: the inner `kubectl wait` was the real gate | Delete the dead check rather than repair it — `kubectl wait` already returns non-zero on timeout, which is exactly what the loop wants, so a working outer test would only duplicate it. General form: `\|\| true` on a **condition** does not make it safe, it makes it meaningless; use it on statements, never on the thing being tested. The same audit measured the other 14 sites on a live cluster and found the failure rate tracks upstream output size — `kubectl get pods -A \| grep -q` 29/40, `kubectl get netpol -A` 2/40, `ip -o addr show` 1/40, `echo "$VAR" \|` 0/40 (a builtin finishes before grep exits). | 2026-08-05 | `WARN: chrony service not found after install` on a node where `chrony.service` was installed, enabled, and `NTPSynchronized=yes`. The test was `systemctl list-unit-files \| grep -q "^chrony\.service"`: `grep -q` exits on the first match, closing the pipe while systemctl is still writing its long unit table, systemctl takes SIGPIPE, and `set -o pipefail` turns rc=141 into a false negative. Measured 37/40 runs wrong. Second sighting of the pipefail/SIGPIPE trap (2026-08-04 was `apt-cache madison \| awk exit`, which killed all six nodes) — but that one died loudly, and this one did not | `systemctl cat "${c}.service"` — 0/40 wrong, no pipe at all. The lesson is the **inside-`if`** variant of the trap: at statement level pipefail kills the script with no message, but inside a condition nothing dies, the branch just doesn't run. Here everything downstream sat inside `if [ -n "${CHRONY_SVC}" ]`, so the false negative silently skipped the `makestep 0.1 -1` fix (D12) and the kubelet clock-sync gate, and the only evidence was a WARN line saying clock sync had been left to the hypervisor. Recovered from git; the giveaway was a section-header lookup failing on a file that should have had five |

Distinguish a transient failure from a broken configuration by probing several times before changing anything — the previous helm failure at this same line WAS configuration (squid started before its resolver), and treating this one the same way would have chased a fix that was already correct |

Mitigations that hold regardless: Phase 2 attempts now genuinely resume (see the Helm 4 row), so a lost attempt is lost wall-clock rather than lost work, and the budget moves 3 → 5. `up.sh` milestones now carry timestamps — the reason this could not be correlated against the guests' journals is that vagrant's output has no clock, so the drop was visible in the log and invisible in time |
| 2026-08-04 | `K8S_PATCH_VERSION` looked like a pin and pinned nothing. Worse, `scripts/airgap/01-generate-image-list.sh` parses that same constant out of the Vagrantfile, so the offline bundle was assembled for a version nothing runs | Resolve the apt version by **prefix** (`1.35.7-1.1` — the packaging suffix changes without the Kubernetes version changing) and fail with the available list when the requested patch is not offered; `--allow-downgrades` is required or a node that already took a newer patch refuses to come back and the fleet ends up mixed. It named `platform-system` twice while that namespace demonstrably existed, and it is the first line anyone reads when diagnosing a failed Phase 2 | Fetch the list once and decide locally, so **"could not ask" and "the answer is no" produce different messages**. Any check that collapses both into one exit code will eventually send its reader after the wrong thing; that cost real minutes here, hunting a namespace that was present all along |

Nine more checks had the same shape: their pattern appeared in both code and comments. The same discriminator applies to a JSON Schema sample: a real envelope can validate while a relaxed or bypassed validator accepts an unsupported `schema_version` too. | Anchor positive greps to non-comment lines (`grep -qE '^[^#]*PAT'`), and mutation-test the suite — delete the line each check matches and confirm the check fails. For a schema/CLI guard, copy the committed real event to a temp file, mutate one validity-critical field, and require rejection. The one failure guaranteed to follow a rebuild left no evidence at all | Drop the stale known_hosts entries for the nodes before connecting, and capture ssh's stderr to a file so a failure can print it. | 2026-08-02 | Checked whether phase 2 was still running with `pgrep -f 06-phase2-start` over ssh. Same shape as the `pkill -f '6443:127.0.0.1:6443'` that killed its own invocation earlier in this work | `pgrep`/`pkill` patterns are matched against every process including the one asking. Confirm from the artefact instead — the log's last line and its mtime said "finished" plainly. This has now bitten three times in one exercise (`pkill -f '6443:…'` killing its own invocation, phase 2 reported RUNNING for 28 hours, and `pgrep -cf` counting the ssh that carried it), so treat any process-name check over ssh as wrong by default |
| 2026-08-01 | Two sessions worked this repo at once and neither noticed. The rows survived intact, so nothing looked wrong; the damage is that `git log` now misdescribes them. Compounding it, `git log` authorship cannot separate the sessions at all: `user.name` is `Antigravity` for every commit in this repo, and a claim was made in review that a commit came from "another agent" on exactly that non-signal | Stage the paths your task touched, never `git add -A`, and re-check `git status` immediately before committing — in a repo with concurrent sessions the tree is not yours alone. If a `grep -c` for your own change returns 0, suspect the pattern before suspecting a revert: `$2` and `(` inside a shell-quoted regex nearly produced a false "my fix was reverted" alarm here — `grep -F` settles it |

Every deletion is `--force --grace-period=0`, so a single mis-evaluated predicate goes straight to irreversible with no ceiling on blast radius. Kin to the 2026-07-30 `registry`-stage row: both report success over a failure they caused | A repair tool must not be its own judge. Bound the damage before trusting the judgment: refuse to delete a whole DaemonSet in one pass, prefer graceful deletion, and make the success threshold something a reader can check (why 3?). Note also what the log looks like when this misfires — `Deleting unhealthy Cilium pod` reads as the healer working, which is why it survived a month unquestioned |

Widening the glob would fail CI on those pre-existing warnings, which is why the gap persists: the cheap fix is blocked by what it would reveal | Widen the lint scope and fix what it surfaces in the same change, or the gap is permanent by construction. When adding a check, confirm which paths it actually covers — `scripts/` looked like "all scripts" to everyone who read the workflow |
| 2026-07-31 | **Three of the four pod filters in `cluster-heal.sh` had the wrong field indices**, and the class failed in both directions. Failing OPEN: the Cilium filter read a `-n kube-system` query with the `-A` layout — `awk '$3!="1/1" && $3!="2/2" \|\| $4!="Running"'` — so it compared STATUS to `"1/1"` and RESTARTS to `"Running"`, both always true, and the next line force-deleted all 6 healthy cilium pods plus the operator. Confirmed once in journald (2026-07-29 23:38:37, one second after the service started); the service has been enabled since 2026-07-01 and runs at every master-1 boot, but journald retains only two boots so earlier runs are unevidenced — an initial "every run for months" claim was not supportable. Failing CLOSED: the istio-system and platform-CrashLoop blocks matched `$4` against STATUS values in single-namespace listings, so neither had deleted anything in its entire life. The `1/1 or 2/2` allowlist was a third defect underneath, since this cluster runs 3/3 and 5/5 pods | `kubectl get pods` shifts every column by one between `-n <ns>` (NAME READY STATUS) and `-A` (NS NAME READY STATUS) — never copy a field index between the two forms, and audit *every* filter in a file once one is found wrong. The fail-closed half is the harder lesson: a block that never fires produces no symptom at all, and its neighbours' log lines get read as proof it works — the "Deleting Unknown Istio pod" entries everyone attributed to the istio block came from the cluster-wide sweep. The 2026-07-30 rule below would have caught the open case: run the checker against a known-good subject and confirm it selects **nothing**. For the closed case the inverse is needed — confirm it selects **something** when you break a subject on purpose |

A check that flags a comment, or normal transient state, teaches its reader to skip it — which costs more than the check ever earns |

Links to other docs were repointed by basename, but every `../` path out of `docs/` was now one level short — and those failed silently because the rewriter had no rule for them | After a move, resolve every link against the filesystem and fail on the ones that do not exist. Rewriting what you predicted will break leaves behind what you did not |
| 2025-01-27 | Script written without `set -e` | Always add `set -euo pipefail` as the first line |
| - | Variable expansion error in heredoc | Use `<<'EOF'` (quoted) to prevent variable expansion, `<<EOF` to allow it |
| - | Missing `-y` flag in apt commands | Always use `apt-get install -y` |

Discriminator: **a script that reads a Secret written by a later script must carry the same default as the writer, ideally from one shared function; `\|\| echo ""` turns "not created yet" into a silently empty credential.** |

A pre-merge review of the first design found: "existing Secret wins" would keep `admin/admin` forever and 07 copied the Velero key into CNPG; two S3 servers are active (the `seaweedfs-s3` Service selects the standalone `s3` Deployment, but `seaweedfs-filer` also publishes an embedded filer S3 on 8333) so enabling auth on one leaves a bypass; the identities file is read once at start (no hot reload); `scripts/test/verify-backup.sh` treated HTTP 403 as success. | `ensure_s3_identities` (`scripts/common/lib.sh`) creates per-client random identities (loki, tempo, velero, cnpg; actions scoped `Read/Write/List/Tagging:<bucket>`, never Admin) from the client Secrets as single source and derives `storage/seaweedfs-s3-config` + Velero's `cloud` file; it replaces only the exact `admin/admin` pair, once, and fails closed (writes nothing) on a half-filled pair, odd characters or any non-NotFound read error. Chart: `s3.enableAuth` + `existingConfigSecret`, embedded filer S3 disabled, Loki/Tempo via envFrom + expand-env, CNPG Secret removed from GitOps (14 now only guards it). `verify-backup.sh` now requires an anonymous 403 (200 = FAIL) and Ready BackupRepositories. R249/R250/R270/R271; staged cutover in `docs/runbooks/seaweedfs-s3-auth.md`. Discriminators: **403 on `GET /` proves nothing about whether signed client requests work - only a real write/read per client does; an "existing value wins" credential helper must also say which existing values are known defaults; a Service can publish a second, differently-configured listener of the same daemon; a file-based identities config needs a restart step decided by comparing the config with what the pods loaded (checksum annotation), not by whether this run wrote a Secret; SeaweedFS keys identities by access key (a shared key hands one client the other's bucket), so access keys must be unique; "Secret exists with empty fields" is corruption, not absence; a restore check must write a random token after the pod runs and use a pod that cannot rewrite it; botocore anonymous access is `signature_version=UNSIGNED`, not the string "unsigned".** Not verified live. |

R251/R252. Discriminator: **a completion gate must read a signal written at the end of the work, not a side effect created in the middle; a namespace existing does not mean the later steps ran.** Existing clusters have no marker, so the first `up.sh` run re-runs the idempotent Phase 2 once. |

### Kubernetes/Helm Mistakes

| Date | Mistake | Fix |
|------|---------|-----|
| 2026-10-19 | Velero RBAC generator denied nodes/events so fs-backup could not run (PVB "cannot get nodes", then stuck Prepared on events) (Narwhal#418). | 2026-08-09 | Prometheus scraped node-exporter on only 1 of 6 Kakao nodes and missed kube-controller-manager (:10257) / kube-scheduler (:10259) entirely, producing 9 of the 15 firing alerts. **The confident first diagnosis was wrong.** A diagnosis lane found ztunnel `io error: deadline has elapsed` in the ambient-enrolled `monitoring` namespace, called it "Confirmed Cause: ztunnel egress interception", and the `istio.io/dataplane-mode: "none"` opt-out was deployed — the recreated pod carried the label and **every target stayed down with identical `context deadline exceeded`**. Real cause: the Kakao security group allows `10251-10252` (the pre-1.17 metric ports, a fossil matching nothing) but not `10257/10259`, and has no `9100` at all; Kakao SGs drop rather than reject, so blocked scrapes look like timeouts, and ztunnel's error was the same drop seen one layer up | Three additive SG rules in `csp/kakao-cloud/terraform/modules/security/main.tf` (9100, 10257, 10259 from the VPC CIDR); `tofu plan` showed 0 add/1 change/0 destroy and all 11 targets recovered within 90s of apply, alerts 15→5. Discriminators that separate SG-drop from ztunnel-intercept — both produce "only the local node works": (1) **deploy the opt-out and re-test** — if the opted-out pod still times out, the mesh was never the blocker; (2) kubelet `:10250` scraping cross-node all along — ztunnel does not exempt 10250, but the SG explicitly allows it; (3) timeout-not-refused on a cloud provider whose SGs drop. The ambient opt-out was kept (an in-mesh scraper adds an interception layer for no benefit) but it is hardening, not the fix. "The evidence supports my hypothesis" is not "the evidence rules out the others" — the ztunnel log line was a symptom shared by both theories |

| - | Missing namespace creation | Use `--create-namespace` or `CreateNamespace=true` syncOption |
| - | Ignoring CRD dependencies | Create CR after Operator installation, use ArgoCD sync-wave |
| - | Attempted to resize PVC | PVC can only be expanded, not shrunk |
| 2026-01-30 | OAuth2 Proxy cookie-secret empty value | `cookieSecret: ""` causes error, generate exactly 32 bytes with `openssl rand -hex 16` |
| 2026-01-30 | OAuth2 Proxy service.port deprecated | Use `service.portNumber` (v7.x chart) |
| 2026-01-30 | Keycloak issuer URL mismatch | `insecure_oidc_skip_issuer_verification = true` or match Keycloak hostname setting |
| 2026-01-30 | Loki chunks/results cache failure | `chunksCache.enabled: false`, `resultsCache.enabled: false` for dev environment |
| 2026-01-30 | Headlamp Helm repo URL changed | Use `https://kubernetes-sigs.github.io/headlamp/` |
| 2026-01-30 | Keycloak service name typo | `keycloak-service` (not `keycloak`), specify port 8080 |
| 2026-02-04 | YAML parsing error when modifying with sed | Use `yq` for safe YAML modification (e.g., API server manifest OIDC config) |
| 2026-02-05 | Helm `--set` nodeSelector boolean error | Use `--set-string nodeSelector.key=true` (force string) |
| 2026-02-14 | kube-vip `vip_subnet` value contains `/` | Use `"32"` (NOT `"/32"`), internally combines `address + "/" + vip_subnet` |
| 2026-02-14 | kube-vip kubeconfig path not recognized | Mount at `/.kube/config` (distroless image HOME=/) |
| 2026-02-14 | kube-vip admin.conf VIP circular dependency | Create separate `kube-vip.conf`, change server address to local IP |
| 2026-02-14 | kube-vip static pod created before init (chicken-and-egg) | master-1 creates manifest after init, bootstrap with manual VIP binding |
| 2026-02-14 | kubeadm join detects VMware NAT interface | `--apiserver-advertise-address=192.168.56.x` must be specified |
| 2026-02-14 | VMware Vagrant private_network IP not assigned | Create netplan directly in `01-prerequisites.sh`, `chmod 600` required |
| 2026-02-14 | API server OOM restart on Master 4GB RAM (during platform apps installation) | Current topology: Master has NoSchedule taint (control-plane only, 4GB OK), Worker 6GB runs platform apps |
| 2026-06-07 | Master 4GB still too tight at steady state: apiserver ~2GB (no mem limit) + mandatory DaemonSets ~0.8GB → ~567Mi headroom on master-3 → apiserver SIGKILL (exit 137 via /livez timeout) + intermittent NodeNotReady flapping (also broke metrics-server → portal CPU/mem blank) | **Fixed**: bumped `MASTER_MEMORY` 4096→6144 in Vagrantfile. Apply: rolling `vagrant reload` one master at a time. Partial: exclude falco DaemonSet from masters |
| 2026-02-14 | ArgoCD v3.x applicationsets CRD exceeds 262KB | Use `kubectl apply --server-side --force-conflicts` |
| 2026-02-14 | Helm `--wait` causes release rollback on timeout | Remove `--wait` for non-critical apps, use `--timeout` only |
| 2026-02-15 | Velero CRD hook musl/glibc incompatibility (ARM64) | Set `upgradeCRDs: false`, alpine/k8s musl cannot execute in velero glibc container |
| 2026-02-15 | registry.k8s.io/kubectl is distroless (no shell) | Use `docker.io/alpine/k8s:1.31.4` (includes shell+kubectl) |
| 2026-02-15 | Traefik routes applied before GatewayClass created | Wait loop for Traefik deployment+GatewayClass before applying routes |
| 2026-02-15 | etcd container cannot execute `sh -c` | etcd is distroless, call `kubectl exec -- etcdctl ...` directly |
| 2026-02-15 | alpine/k8s image tag has no `v` prefix | Use `"1.31.4"` (NOT `v1.31.4`), verify Docker Hub tag format |
| 2026-02-15 | ArgoCD v3.x `server.insecure` wrong location | Must set in `argocd-cmd-params-cm` (NOT `argocd-cm`). `argocd-cm` is legacy |
| 2026-02-15 | worker/master-2 DNS resolves to public IP | Set `Domains=~local.narwhal.internal` in `systemd-resolved` + use master dnsmasq as DNS |
| 2026-02-15 | Keycloak `groups` scope missing -> `invalid_scope` error | Create realm-level `groups` client scope + add mapper + assign as default scope to all clients |
| 2026-02-15 | ArgoCD SSO `x509: certificate signed by unknown authority` | Add `oidc.tls.insecure.skip.verify: "true"` to `argocd-cm` (self-signed cert) |
| 2026-02-15 | Headlamp `extraArgs` ignored at root level | Must place in `config.extraArgs` for container args injection |
| 2026-02-15 | Harbor `configureUserSettings` change rejected by API | Injected via env vars so cannot modify via API, change Helm values + redeploy |
| 2026-02-18 | Cilium + Istio CNI conflict (cni.exclusive defaults to true) | `cni.exclusive=false` required, prevents Cilium from deleting other CNI configs |
| 2026-02-18 | Cilium socket LB bypasses ztunnel traffic | `socketLB.hostNamespaceOnly=true` required, prevents mesh traffic from bypassing ztunnel |
| 2026-02-18 | Istio CRD annotation exceeds 262KB (ArgoCD) | Add `ServerSideApply=true` to ArgoCD syncOptions |
| 2026-02-18 | Istio 1.28 does not support K8s 1.35 | Use Istio 1.29.x (supports K8s 1.31~1.35) |
| 2026-02-18 | Istio images docker.io only (no alternative) | Docker OSS rate limit exempt, `docker.io/istio/*` usage unavoidable |
| 2026-02-19 | CiliumClusterwideNetworkPolicy `endpointSelector: {}` blocks all traffic | Empty endpointSelector selects all pods -> deny-by-default activated. Never use empty selector in CCNP |
| 2026-02-19 | CoreDNS `forward . /etc/resolv.conf` dnsmasq loop | Master's resolv.conf is 127.0.0.1 (dnsmasq) -> CoreDNS internal loop. Use `forward . 8.8.8.8 8.8.4.4` explicitly |
| 2026-02-19 | Istio ambient HBONE port 15008 blocked by NetworkPolicy | Ambient mesh uses HBONE (15008) between pods. Add TCP/15008 to every existing port-limited ingress rule so its `from` peers remain enforced; a separate peer-less HBONE rule defeats source restrictions. A deny-all workload with no inbound callers, such as the tuning Job, needs no HBONE ingress |
| 2026-09-28 | Namespace-wide ambient allow-hbone caused a cluster-wide Kyverno webhook outage | The lead's live mitigation used `podSelector: {}` in `platform-system` and `devtools`, which selected and ingress-isolated every pod in namespaces that had no existing namespace-wide ingress isolation; apiserver/remote-node to Kyverno `:9443` was denied, so fail-closed admission blocked pod creation cluster-wide for about nine minutes. Add HBONE only inside existing peer-restricted rules, and treat any new namespace-wide selector as a security-boundary change |

| 2026-02-19 | Traefik Gateway API CRD field manager conflict | Extract chart CRDs -> apply with `--server-side --force-conflicts` -> `helm install --skip-crds` |
| 2026-02-24 | Keycloak Operator auto-generated NetworkPolicy missing HBONE 15008 | Operator manages `keycloak-network-policy` so cannot modify directly. Create separate `keycloak-allow-hbone` NetworkPolicy for 15008/TCP (see `11-keycloak.sh` pattern) |

| 2026-02-24 | yq inserts shell quotes into URL -> API server crash | Use `yq -i ".spec... += [\"--flag=value\"]"` (NOT `'...'`). Use double quotes for outer quoting when shell variable expansion needed |
| 2026-02-24 | HTTPRoute does not exist when adding API server OIDC flags | Must create Keycloak HTTPRoute before OIDC verification in `11-keycloak.sh`. Runs before GitOps bootstrap (14) |
| 2026-02-25 | Keycloak OIDC token `aud` claim only contains `account` (K8s API server Unauthorized) | Must add audience mapper to Keycloak `kubernetes` client: `oidc-audience-mapper`, `included.client.audience=kubernetes`. Without it: `oidc: expected audience "kubernetes" got ["account"]` error |
| 2026-02-25 | API server `--oidc-ca-file` missing -> JWKS verification fails with self-signed cert | `--oidc-ca-file=/etc/kubernetes/pki/oidc-ca.crt` required when Keycloak uses self-signed cert. Extract cert: `openssl s_client -connect ... \| openssl x509 -outform PEM` |
| 2026-02-25 | `kcadm.sh --format csv --noquotes \| tail -1` returns wrong ID | CSV format has unstable result order/fields. Use `kcadm.sh get ... 2>/dev/null \| jq -r '.[] \| select(.name=="X") \| .id'` instead |
| 2026-02-25 | `kubectl --token=X` does not override kubeconfig client-cert | X.509 auth takes priority over token. For OIDC testing use `KUBECONFIG=/dev/null kubectl --server=... --certificate-authority=... --token=...` |
| 2026-02-25 | Istio ambient mesh corrupts SSO web server cookies -> `http: named cookie not present` | Web servers in ambient namespace (ArgoCD, Grafana, Harbor, Gitea, OAuth2-Proxy, Headlamp) must opt-out with `istio.io/dataplane-mode: none` pod label. ztunnel HBONE corrupts Set-Cookie/Cookie headers |
| 2026-02-25 | Gitea OAuth2 source name case mismatch -> `/user/oauth2/Keycloak` 500 error | URL path source name is case-sensitive. Register with `gitea admin auth add-oauth --name "keycloak"` (lowercase) to access via `/user/oauth2/keycloak` |
| 2026-02-25 | `microprofile-jwt` scope has duplicate `groups` claim mapper | Default Keycloak `microprofile-jwt` scope already maps realm-role to `groups`. After creating custom `groups` scope, delete the `groups` mapper from other scopes |

| 2026-02-26 | Keycloak user `emailVerified=false` -> OAuth2-Proxy 500 error | Add `-s emailVerified=true` to `kcadm.sh create users`. Or add `insecure_oidc_allow_unverified_email = true` to OAuth2-Proxy |
| 2026-02-26 | Traefik Errors middleware preserves 401 status code -> browser auto-redirect fails | Use nginx+JS redirect page instead of ExternalName->OAuth2-Proxy. Force redirect with `window.location.href` |
| 2026-02-26 | Traefik blocks ExternalName services by default | `providers.kubernetesCRD.allowExternalNameServices: true` required. Without it: 404 `externalName services not allowed` |
| 2026-02-26 | OAuth2-Proxy PKCE conflict (concurrent multiple OAuth flows) | Multiple protected apps redirect simultaneously -> code_verifier conflict. Add sessionStorage 5-second debounce in JS |
| 2026-02-26 | Traefik LB annotation `io.cilium/lb-ipam-ips` ignored by MetalLB | For MetalLB use: `metallb.universe.tf/loadBalancerIPs: "IP"` |

(Contrary to the older note: in 26.6.1 podTemplate **probes ARE honored**; it's **resources** that are dropped.) | Put CPU/memory in the **first-class `Keycloak.spec.resources`** (operator honors it — verified pod got `cpu req 600m`). Relax probes via `unsupported.podTemplate` containers[0] `livenessProbe/readinessProbe/startupProbe` (`timeoutSeconds: 5`, higher `failureThreshold`) — honored. Immediate recovery: `kubectl cordon <hot-node> && kubectl delete pod keycloak-0 -n iam` to reschedule onto a node with CPU headroom, then uncordon. |

Cause: velero-api (Node/passport-oauth2) makes a **server-to-server** call to the Keycloak token endpoint and rejected the private-CA TLS cert (`UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE`). Node trusts only the public bundle — mounting the CA for the *browser* path is irrelevant. Also: velero-ui SPA only completes the OAuth code exchange for a `state` matching `localStorage['auth.oauth.state']` → a cross-origin Keycloak authorize deep link can't work; zero-click requires a SAME-ORIGIN bootstrap page that seeds that key first (APISIX `serverless-pre-function` `/sso` route). Same pattern for OpenBao: `/sso` + `/sso/callback` pages drive the OIDC API flow and write the token as `localStorage['vault-token☃1']` in the UI's format (needs the extra redirect URI in BOTH the oidc role and the Keycloak client) | Mount `narwhal-ca-cert` secret + set `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS=/etc/narwhal-ca/ca.crt` in chart values (`gitops/apps/velero-ui.yaml`). Verify in-pod: `node -e 'fetch(tokenUrl,{method:"POST"})'` → HTTP 400 (TLS ok) not TLS error. Deploy via Gitea push (kubectl apply gets reverted by app-of-apps selfHeal). Zero-click bootstrap routes: `apisix-routes.yaml` `openbao-sso`/`velero-ui-sso`; APISIX IC may need a restart to translate new ApisixRoute rules |

ALSO: Keycloak 24+ **silently drops** user attributes set via admin API unless the realm user-profile allows them (`unmanagedAttributePolicy` unset = blocked) — a protocol mapper for the attribute then emits nothing and Harbor falls back to the `name` claim (onboards as `admin_narwhal` from full name "admin narwhal") | Dedicated username claim: realm user-profile `unmanagedAttributePolicy=ENABLED` (11-2), per-user attribute `harbor_username` (admin→`narwhal-admin`, others→own username), `oidc-usermodel-attribute-mapper` on the harbor client, Harbor `oidc_user_claim=harbor_username` (11-3).
