| 2026-07-10 | Kyverno Enforce validate pattern WITHOUT `=()` optional anchors (`securityContext: privileged: "!true"`) requires the field to EXIST → every pod lacking an explicit `privileged` field (i.e. most pods) was denied at admission in non-excluded namespaces; surfaced as velero-ui ReplicaSet `FailedCreate`, and would have blocked ALL rollouts/reschedules (reboot = cluster-wide outage) | In Kyverno patterns, always wrap optional fields in `=()` anchors: `=(securityContext): =(privileged): "false"` (upstream-standard form, also cover `=(initContainers)`/`=(ephemeralContainers)`). Verify both directions with `kubectl run --dry-run=server`: plain pod admitted AND privileged pod denied. (e898f59) |
| 2026-07-13 | GitOps portal pin bumps silently NOT deploying while the app showed Synced: argocd-cm carried a GLOBAL `ignoreDifferences.apps_Deployment` ignoring the narwhal-portal container `.image`/`.resources` (added for skaffold live-dev so selfHeal wouldn't revert dev images). With immutable SemVer pins this made every image-only bump invisible — bumps only landed by piggybacking on unrelated changes in the same sync. Extra trap: argocd-cm has a self-ignore on its own `.data` customization keys, so fixing the rule in git alone never reaches the live cm — must ALSO `kubectl patch` argocd-cm | Make the ignore CONDITIONAL on a dev image actually running: `select(.name=="narwhal-portal") \| select(.image \| test(":dev-")) \| .image` (and same for `.resources`) in `argocd-config.yaml` + live-patch argocd-cm. Verified: 1.0.14 pin synced in 10s after the patch. When an app is Synced but live differs from git, grep argocd-cm `resource.customizations.ignoreDifferences` before blaming sync machinery |
| 2026-07-13 | Recurring "argocd-redis EOF wedge" root-caused: `13-argocd.sh` opted server/repo-server/controller/notifications out of ambient but NOT `argocd-redis` → redis stayed mesh-enrolled while all its clients are non-mesh → under STRICT mTLS ztunnel drops their plaintext connections → repo-server "failed to list refs: EOF" / "retrieve git references from cache: EOF", Gitea-sourced apps stuck Unknown, gitops pin bumps never sync. Restarting redis/repo-server only helped while ztunnel enforcement flapped — fresh pods failed identically | Add `istio.io/dataplane-mode: none` to the argocd-redis Deployment pod template (13-argocd.sh + live patch). Verified: repo-server EOF 68/2min → 0 immediately. Same non-mesh-client→enrolled-server pattern as the STRICT mTLS exceptions lesson — when opting an app's clients out of ambient, opt out its backing services too |
| 2026-07-13 | Portal login → 502 on `/api/auth/callback/keycloak`: portal 1.0.11 started storing Keycloak access+refresh tokens in the NextAuth JWE → session cookie chunked into 2+ ~4KB `Set-Cookie` headers → response headers exceeded nginx's default `proxy_buffer_size 4k` → APISIX "upstream sent too big header" → 502; the browser retry then hit `invalid_grant: Code not valid` (code already consumed). Initially misdiagnosed as a Keycloak-restart blip — the APISIX **error** log (not access log) had the real cause | Raise proxy buffers in apisix `fullCustomConfig` → `nginx_config.http_configuration_snippet`: `proxy_buffer_size 16k; proxy_buffers 8 16k; proxy_busy_buffers_size 32k;`. For any gateway 502 on an auth callback, check APISIX error log for "too big header" FIRST. Verify with a scripted curl login (csrf→signin→Keycloak form→callback) expecting 302 + chunked session cookies |
| 2026-07-10 | Added a SECOND `image:` block to an ArgoCD Application inline `values:` string (helm values YAML) — duplicate map key silently resolved last-wins → the original block's `tag: "0.10.1"` was dropped, image fell back to chart appVersion (0.10.0 rollback). Also: kyverno `add-default-securitycontext` mutate injects `runAsNonRoot: true`; images with a non-numeric USER (e.g. `node`) then fail kubelet verification (`CreateContainerConfigError`) — fix by setting numeric `runAsUser` in values | Before adding a key to a Helm values block, grep the block for an existing key and MERGE into it; validate rendered output (`helm template` + check the final image tag), not just YAML syntax. For mutate-injected `runAsNonRoot`, pair it with explicit `runAsUser: <uid>` when the image USER is non-numeric. (f1d7333) |
| 2026-09-08 | (narwhal-portal#20) `13-2-narwhal-portal-bindings.sh` unconditionally minted an 8760h `kubectl create token` and injected it as a static `K8S_SA_TOKEN` bearer secret into `narwhal-portal-secrets` — same class of gap as #156's OPENBAO_TOKEN before that fix: a year-long static credential with no rotation, always present regardless of whether the portal's file-based token reader (`src/lib/k8s-token.ts`) even needed it. The pod's own `automountServiceAccountToken` (default true, never disabled) also meant a second explicit volumeMount at the standard `/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount` path would have collided with kubelet's own automount at that same path — the fix had to use a dedicated mount path, not just "add a projected volume" | Added a `k8s-api-token` projected `serviceAccountToken` volume (1h TTL, audience `https://kubernetes.default.svc.cluster.local` — kubeadm's default issuer, confirmed no `--service-account-issuer` override in `02-init-cluster.sh`) mounted at a DEDICATED path (`/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount-k8s-api`, not the automount's standard path) with `K8S_SA_TOKEN_FILE`/`K8S_TOKEN_AUDIENCE` env pointing at it. Gated the legacy static token behind `ENABLE_LEGACY_K8S_SA_TOKEN=true` (default false, loud WARN), mirroring `ENABLE_LEGACY_OPENBAO_TOKEN`. R147/R147b/R148/R148b in `regression-check-kakao.sh` guard both halves. **Mistake made while fixing**: R148b's drift check first used an unescaped `${K8S_SA_TOKEN}` inside a double-quoted `python3 -c "..."` block that also needed `${r148_drift_tmp}` bash-interpolated — bash silently expanded the unset `K8S_SA_TOKEN` to empty before python ever saw it, so the assert would have compared against the wrong string. Discriminator: whenever a regression-check python/sed one-liner needs ONE bash variable interpolated (a temp path) but must also contain LITERAL shell-variable-shaped text from the target file (`${SOME_VAR}`), every `$` in the literal text must be escaped (`\$`) or the check silently validates nothing — caught by comparing against R136b's exact same construct rather than trusting the first draft to run correctly |

### GitOps/ArgoCD Mistakes

| Date | Mistake | Fix |
|------|---------|-----|
| 2026-09-28 | (고치다 만든 실수) #252를 되돌리면서 Deployment의 `GITEA_URL=https://gitea.${DOMAIN}` env까지 지우자, 새 포털 파드의 readiness가 503(`GITEA_URL (must be https://)`)이 됐다. 14가 Secret에 넣는 값은 ArgoCD용 내부 `http://gitea-http...:3000`이었고, 포털 1.0.19는 production에서 https를 강제한다. 즉 중복이라고 판단한 env가 사실은 이 불일치를 가리고 있었다. 판별자: 같은 키를 두 곳(envFrom Secret, Deployment env)에서 줄 때는 우선순위가 높은 쪽이 다른 쪽의 결함을 숨긴다. 되돌리기 전에 가려진 쪽의 값만으로 기동해 봐야 한다. | 소유자인 14가 포털 Secret에는 `PORTAL_GITEA_URL=https://gitea.${DOMAIN}`을 쓰고, ArgoCD 리포지터리 크리덴셜은 내부 http를 유지한다. R220(포털 GITEA_URL은 https)을 mutation 검증과 함께 추가했다. 라이브에서 Deployment env 없이 새 파드가 Ready였고 의존성 6개가 모두 ok였다. |
| 2026-09-28 | (고치다 만든 실수) #241이 추가한 `ambient-kubelet-probes-ccnp` Application은 클러스터 범위 리소스만 관리해서 `destination.namespace`를 비워 두었다. `platform` AppProject가 빈 namespace를 허용하지 않아 라이브에서 `InvalidSpecError: ... namespace '' do not match any of the allowed destinations`로 한 번도 동기화되지 않았다. 정적 검사 `check-appproject-fit.py`는 빈 namespace를 `if ns`로 걸러 검사하지 않았고, 구현 레인은 clusterResourceWhitelist의 와일드카드만 보고 "권한 충분"이라고 결론냈다. 판별자: 클러스터 범위 전용 Application도 destination은 검사 대상이다. whitelist(무엇을)와 destinations(어디에)는 별개다. | destination에 프로젝트가 허용하는 namespace(`kube-system`)를 지정했다. 기존 클러스터 범위 앱인 kyverno-policies도 `devtools`를 쓴다. 검사기는 빈 destination namespace를 위반으로 보고하며, 수정 전 트리에서 이 앱을 검출하는 것을 확인했다. 라이브에서 앱이 Synced가 되는 것까지 확인한다. |
| 2026-09-28 | Narwhal#242: Gitea chart uploads were a critical prerequisite for app-of-apps, but transient 503 responses only emitted WARN; the script exited successfully and Helm Applications remained Unknown. | Wait for registry readiness, retry transient 5xx/000 responses, and fail the provisioning step if any chart remains unpublished. Discriminator: a critical dependency step downgraded to WARN. |
| 2026-09-06 | Issue #52 D4-A asked for a "rollback" mode in `push-to-gitea.sh`, and the naive read is a `git reset --hard`/force-push of `main` back to an old commit. That would both violate the audit-trail intent and get rejected outright: `main` is selfHeal-tracked (a reset gets immediately re-applied to whatever ArgoCD last saw) and branch-protected with a push whitelist of only `gitea-admin` (`14-gitops-bootstrap.sh`), which does not special-case force pushes. A second near-miss: a "release tag" that can later be re-pointed to a different commit is not actually a release — it just moves the ambiguity from "which commit" to "which tag". (고치다 만든 실수, found by an independent review of the first cut) The dirty-`gitops/`-tree safety gate was written as `git -C "${REPO_ROOT}" diff/status ...` unconditionally, which assumes the script always runs from a git checkout. It doesn't: `Vagrantfile` rsyncs `scripts/` alone to `/home/vagrant/scripts` with no `.git` anywhere above it, so on a node `REPO_ROOT=/home/vagrant`, `git -C` exits 128, the guard's `!` inverted that into "dirty", and `--rollback` died with "gitops/ has uncommitted changes" on exactly the disaster-recovery path (`docs/vagrant/disaster-recovery.md` step 5) that exists to run `--rollback` when things are already broken. Separately, `--tag`'s remote-tag-exists check ran AFTER the publish commit+push, so re-tagging an existing version still created a new `main` commit before exiting 1 | Implemented `--tag <ver>` (annotated tag on the just-published commit; existence check now runs immediately after clone, before any commit/push — refuses if the tag already exists on the remote with zero new commits, tags are never moved) and `--rollback <ver>` (verifies the tag exists on the remote, then publishes that tag's tree as a **new forward commit** — `git read-tree --reset -u <tag>^{tree}` + commit + push — never a reset/force-push). The dirty-tree gate now only runs when `git -C "${REPO_ROOT}" rev-parse --show-toplevel` succeeds AND resolves to a path containing `${GITOPS_DIR}` (symlink-normalized via `pwd -P`, not just the literal `gitops` pathspec); otherwise it logs one line ("skipping local-dirty guard: ... is not a git checkout ... source is an rsync copy") and continues, since a rollback only needs the remote tag, never the local tree. Verified against a throwaway `git init --bare` repo: after publish → tag v0.0.1 → publish a change → rollback v0.0.1, `main` has exactly 3 commits (never rewritten) and `git ls-tree -r main` is byte-identical to `git ls-tree -r v0.0.1`; a repeated `--tag v0.0.1` exits 1 with the commit count unchanged; `--rollback` with a dirty `gitops/` inside a real checkout exits 1; running the same rollback from a copy of the script placed OUTSIDE any git checkout (simulating the node's rsynced `scripts/`) now exits 0 instead of the old false "uncommitted changes" failure. Discriminator: a guard that assumes the script runs from a checkout is wrong on the node, where only `scripts/` is rsynced — test the DR path from a non-repo directory, not just from the repo you wrote the guard in; separately, rollback in a selfHeal GitOps system is a forward commit that reproduces an immutable tag's tree, not a branch rewrite, and any "does X already exist" check for an immutable resource must run before the mutation it's supposed to prevent, not after |
| 2026-08-09 | The 2026-08-05 three-part Gitea-registry lesson (below) **re-manifested wholesale on Kakao four days after being "fixed"**, because all three parts lived in repo HEAD and none had reached this cluster: the Helm package registry was EMPTY (`entries: {}`), `argocd-tls-certs-cm` had zero keys, the `narwhal-charts-repo` Secret did not exist, and the live `gitea-git-bypass` regex lacked `^/api/packages/`. Yet every app read **Synced** for 4 days and prometheus-stack had deployed 86.2.3 successfully — the ArgoCD repo-server chart cache answered for the empty registry, exactly like the registry blob-cache incident of 08-06. The first hard refresh tore the mask off: `ComparisonError: chart not found`, then `x509: unknown authority`, then `gzip: invalid header` (the OIDC login page served where a tarball should be), one hidden layer per fix. Root of the CA half: `13-argocd.sh` guards CA population with `\|\| true` when the wildcard cert is not yet issued — on Kakao it was not, the block silently skipped, and nothing ever re-ran it | Republished the 27 bundled charts, populated the cm (both hostname keys) and repo Secret per `13-argocd.sh`, pushed the bypass-regex file to gitea. Three discriminators worth keeping: (1) **`Synced` means "matches the in-cluster gitea copy", never "matches repo HEAD"** — the two drift independently per cluster, so verify a fix reached a given cluster by reading its live object, not by repo grep; (2) a cache in front of a registry makes emptiness invisible until refresh — after any registry repair, hard-refresh one chart-sourced app as the acceptance test (the 08-05 row's own advice, not followed on this cluster); (3) an install-time `\|\| true` guard on a dependency that "is not ready yet" needs a later step that actually re-runs it, or it is a silent permanent skip. Sequenced errors are also a signal: each fix revealing a *different* error is progress through stacked causes, not a failing fix |
| 2026-08-09 | UI in portal compliance tab reported "waiting for Trivy Operator scan" permanently for KISA framework. Assumed trivy-operator scan was pending, but no KISA ClusterComplianceReport CRD resource existed on the cluster | Created `ClusterComplianceReport` for KISA (`gitops/resources/kisa-compliance.yaml`) mapping KISA controls to real Trivy check IDs (`AVD-KCV-*`, `AVD-KSV-*`) and wired via ArgoCD (`gitops/charts/narwhal-apps/templates/kisa-compliance.yaml`). Discriminator: A UI saying "waiting for scan" can mean the underlying custom resource was never created at all — list CRDs (`kubectl get clustercompliancereports`) to distinguish uncreated resources from delayed scan jobs |
| 2026-07-31 → 08-04 | **`accounts.narwhal-portal` did not exist when `13-2-narwhal-portal-bindings.sh` asked ArgoCD for a token, so the failure was deterministic — and it was twice misread as a timeout.** The token endpoint answered `account 'narwhal-portal' does not exist`; admin login succeeded, which is why the retry loop looked like it was almost working. The account's only declaration lives in `gitops/charts/narwhal-platform/templates/argocd-config.yaml`, which ArgoCD applies at `14-gitops-bootstrap.sh` — one step AFTER the script that needs it — while 13-2's own header comment claimed 13-argocd.sh created it. The script then wrote `REPLACE_ME__argocd_token` into narwhal-portal-secrets, the portal caught the 401, logged `Connection failed, returning empty` and rendered an empty widget, so the cluster read as healthy | 13-argocd.sh now writes `accounts.narwhal-portal: apiKey,login` into argocd-cm with the value the chart uses, so the later GitOps sync is a no-op. **The diagnostic lesson is the expensive one:** it "worked" whenever anyone re-ran 13-2 by hand, because by then GitOps had caught up — which made it look like a timing problem and got it "fixed" twice by waiting longer (12×10s → 30×10s). Read the API's own error before tuning a timeout; `{"error":"account ... does not exist"}` was there the whole time and was being discarded. What did survive from those attempts is worth keeping: verify the token authenticates (200) before storing it, and store nothing if it does not — a placeholder and an absent key break the portal identically, and only one lies about it |
| 2026-07-07 | Portal deployed via a `:latest` image tag in the GitOps manifest — ArgoCD compares the rendered manifest string, so a re-published `:latest` is an IDENTICAL manifest → **no diff detected → no sync → stale image runs** until a manual `rollout restart`; selfHeal never fires either. (D-portal-ghcr) | Pin an immutable SemVer tag in GitOps (`ghcr.io/dasomel/narwhal-portal:1.0.0`). Upgrade = bump the tag → ArgoCD sees the diff → auto-syncs. Reserve `:latest` for non-GitOps flows. Portal now pulls the pinned public GHCR image; in-cluster Kaniko build demoted to optional dev tool (`docs/common/developer-kaniko-builds.md`) |
| 2026-07-05 | `scripts/gitops/push-to-gitea.sh` uses `cp -r` (mirror or per-path) into a fresh Gitea clone — this only adds/overwrites files, it never deletes a file that no longer exists in the local `gitops/` tree. Renaming/removing a gitops Application file (e.g. `promtail.yaml` -> `k8s-monitoring.yaml` for the Alloy migration) and running the script as-is would leave the stale old file committed in Gitea alongside the new one -> two conflicting ArgoCD Applications | For a rename/delete, do the Gitea clone/push manually with an explicit `git rm` instead of relying on the script's plain `cp -r` |
| 2026-07-10 | Ran `git push -f gitea main` from the narwhal repo DIRECTLY to the in-cluster `narwhal-gitops` Gitea repo — the two have DIFFERENT tree structures (gitea root = CONTENTS of `gitops/`), so the force-push replaced Gitea main with the entire narwhal repo tree (Vagrantfile, scripts/, docs/ …); a later `push-to-gitea.sh` mirror superimposed the correct root so ArgoCD kept working, masking the pollution. Also `git remote set-url` embedded the gitea-admin PASSWORD in plaintext in `.git/config` | NEVER push the narwhal repo directly to the gitea remote — always go through `scripts/gitops/push-to-gitea.sh` (it clones fresh and maps `gitops/` -> repo root). Cleanup was a clone + `git rm -r` of the non-gitops paths + normal push. Never embed credentials in a git remote URL; the script reads the password from the cluster secret per-run |
| - | values file path typo | `valueFiles` paths are relative to repoURL |
| - | targetRevision format error | Chart version is `"1.0.0"` (string), Git ref is `HEAD` |
| 2026-02-14 | app-of-apps repoURL uses `https://` -> Gitea is HTTP only | Use `http://gitea-http.gitea.svc.cluster.local:3000/...` |
| 2026-02-14 | Harbor gitops YAML missing ARM64 image overrides | Must specify all components: `ghcr.io/dasomel/goharbor/*:latest` |
| 2026-02-14 | SeaweedFS chart version 4.0.410 -> Docker Hub image 4.10 doesn't exist | Verify appVersion=image tag exists before using (4.0.407->4.07) |
| 2026-02-14 | ArgoCD repo-server GitHub Pages IPv6 connection failure | VM does not support IPv6, ArgoCD fails when attempting IPv6 |
| 2026-02-14 | ArgoCD managed resource Helm upgrade conflict | Patch directly with `kubectl set image`, use kubectl instead of Helm |
| 2026-02-15 | Headlamp v0.40.0 has no `-oidc-skip-issuer-tls-verify` flag | Mount CA cert to `/etc/ssl/certs/` with subPath, do not use `SSL_CERT_FILE` |
| 2026-02-15 | Grafana `assertNoLeakedSecrets` chart validation failure | `grafana.assertNoLeakedSecrets: false` required |
| 2026-02-15 | Prometheus Helm release name mismatch (`prometheus` vs `prometheus-stack`) | Must match ArgoCD app name for HTTPRoute/ConfigMap to work correctly |
| 2026-02-15 | Gitea OIDC source add fails with self-signed cert verification | Mount CA cert in Gitea container before running `add-oauth` |
| 2026-02-24 | ArgoCD installed in non-default namespace (devtools) ClusterRoleBinding Subject mismatch | Upstream install.yaml always sets Subject namespace to `argocd`. After installation, `kubectl patch clusterrolebinding argocd-application-controller argocd-applicationset-controller argocd-server` to update namespace to actual installation namespace |
| 2026-02-24 | Istio ambient namespace ArgoCD health check ports (8082/8084/9001) blocked by ztunnel | ztunnel intercepts all inbound traffic in ambient namespace. kubelet probe (plain HTTP) conflicts with ztunnel expecting mTLS -> CrashLoopBackOff. Add `istio.io/dataplane-mode: none` label to pod template to exclude from ambient. `traffic.sidecar.istio.io/excludeInboundPorts` annotation does not work in ambient mode |
| 2026-02-26 | ArgoCD selfHeal reverts kubectl apply changes | ArgoCD managed resources revert to Gitea state even when modified with kubectl. Must push to Gitea repo for persistence |
| 2026-02-26 | Gitea headless service (ClusterIP: None) -> git clone fails | DNS resolution fails with headless Gitea service. Use pod IP directly: `kubectl get pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=gitea -o jsonpath='{.items[0].status.podIP}'` |
| 2026-06-11 | APISIX 3.16 has NO `kubernetes` secret manager (only vault/aws/gcp) — `$secret://kubernetes/k8s-1/...` ApisixRoute refs can never resolve and `secret_providers` in config.yaml is not a real APISIX option. IC meanwhile DID sync ExternalName-backed routes, creating broken duplicates that shadowed the working manual admin-API routes → velero-ui (and 5 others) `unauthorized_client` → callback 500 | Inject OIDC secrets via apisix chart `extraEnvVars` (secretKeyRef) + reference `$env://VAR` in ApisixRoute; delete legacy manual duplicate routes from etcd via admin API |
| 2026-10-18 | Narwhal#419: `s3_sync_server_config` rolled `seaweedfs-s3` by patching the pod-template annotation `narwhal.io/s3-config-sha256`, and the Argo CD controller (`selfHeal: true`) reverted it within seconds: the old ReplicaSet came back, nothing crashed, and the checksum never persisted, so a key rotation did not restart the S3 server. The patch itself succeeded, which made the helper look correct. | The seaweedfs Application lists exactly that annotation in `ignoreDifferences` (apps/Deployment storage/seaweedfs-s3, pointer `/spec/template/metadata/annotations/narwhal.io~1s3-config-sha256`) with `RespectIgnoreDifferences=true` (D-s3ops-1; cost: drift of this one field is not repaired; escape hatch: remove both). 판별자: `kubectl get deploy seaweedfs-s3 -o yaml` `managedFields` shows `argocd-controller` `Update` after the patch and the annotation is gone seconds later; a manual `kubectl patch` is not evidence that a GitOps-owned field persists. Argo CD behaviour with the new entry is not yet verified on a live cluster. |
| 2026-10-19 | Narwhal#419 follow-up: after `ignoreDifferences` was added for the roll marker `narwhal.io/s3-config-sha256` the annotation survived self-heal, but the `seaweedfs` Application read OutOfSync. The marker was the only pod-template annotation, so Argo CD's normalized live state kept an empty `annotations: {}` that the desired manifest did not have; the Argo CD API managed-resources diff was exactly that one line. | The S3 pod template now carries the static annotation `narwhal.io/s3-config-source: seaweedfs-s3-config` via `s3.podAnnotations` (D-s3ops-1b; cost: one extra annotation and one S3 roll on the first sync; escape hatch: remove it and the ignoreDifferences entry). 판별자: OutOfSync with a one-line diff on an empty map right after ignoring the only key of that map; fixtures with fakes cannot show this, the Argo CD API diff (`/api/v1/applications/<app>/managed-resources`) did. Applied after merge, not yet verified live. |

### Vagrant/Infrastructure Mistakes

| Date | Mistake | Fix |
|------|---------|-----|
| 2026-08-05 | Migrating those 21 sources to Gitea's Helm registry failed twice more, each time looking like the previous fix had not worked. (1) Gitea publishes `index.yaml` with **absolute** download URLs built from `ROOT_URL`, so ArgoCD — pointed at the in-cluster service — was redirected out to `https://gitea.<domain>/api/packages/…` and met a Keycloak redirect, because that host is behind the APISIX OIDC plugin. A registry is a machine endpoint; a machine cannot complete a browser flow. (2) With the route fixed it still failed on `x509: certificate signed by unknown authority` **even though the CA was already in `argocd-tls-certs-cm`** — repo-server only passes `--ca-file` to the `helm pull` subprocess for repositories it has been *told about* | Three parts, and all three are needed: add `^/api/packages/` to the `gitea-git-bypass` ApisixRoute; put the CA in `argocd-tls-certs-cm` under **both** hostnames (the Application names the service, the redirect lands on the ingress, and the lookup is by configured host); and declare the repo as an `argocd.argoproj.io/secret-type: repository` Secret — without it the ConfigMap is populated and unused, which is the most misleading state of the three. Also `12-gitea.sh` now publishes the bundled `.tgz`s so a clean install populates the registry before the app-of-apps comes up. Verified the way the row below says to: hard refresh on an isolated cluster, 34/34 back to Synced |
| 2026-08-05 | The install went fully offline; **the GitOps layer did not**, and `34/34 Synced Healthy` hid it. 18 ArgoCD `repoURL`s in `gitops/` name public chart repositories (`charts.jetstack.io`, `metallb.github.io`, `github.com/otwld/velero-ui`, …), so every refresh/resync/selfHeal needs the internet. It read as fully Synced because those syncs happened while a default route was still present — the moment isolation was made durable, a forced hard refresh turned harbor, kyverno, metallb, openbao and velero-ui all to `Unknown` with `failed to list refs: ... lookup github.com`. Running workloads were unaffected (161/161), which is exactly why this can sit unnoticed | Never read `Synced` as evidence of anything offline: it describes the last successful comparison, not the ability to make one now. **Force a hard refresh and re-read** — that is the only check that distinguishes "syncs offline" from "synced while online once". Found only because the isolation fix in the row below made the gap persist; with the route silently restored by DHCP, this would have passed every test forever. Fix direction (not yet done): serve the bundled chart `.tgz`s from the in-cluster Harbor as an OCI Helm repo and rewrite the sources, since the charts are already in the bundle. `regression-check` R31 counts the remaining public sources so the number cannot grow while that migration is pending |
| 2026-08-05 | **The once-per-run "SSH drop" was never a drop.** VMware's NAT DHCP gives the same address to two different MACs, and the VMware provider derives each machine's forwarded SSH port *from that address* — so two machines share both, and `vagrant ssh <a>` reaches `<b>`, whose key does not match. Measured live with distinct MACs: master-2 + worker-3 on `172.16.221.135` / port 2204, master-1 + worker-2 on `.133` / port 2203. This is why every in-VM investigation came up empty on 2026-08-04 — nothing happened inside the VM. It also explains the randomness: it depends on which machine boots into the collision, not on what the provisioner was doing. The expensive part was not the failed provision but the **misreport**: `up.sh`'s readiness probe goes through `vagrant ssh master-1`, so it announced `0/6 nodes Ready` for a cluster with 5 Ready nodes and "recovered" a healthy master-1 with a reload | Three changes in `up.sh`. (1) `master1_exec()` reaches master-1 on its static host-only address `192.168.56.10` with that machine's own key — unique per VM, never shared, so the readiness gate cannot be fooled. (2) `nat_ip_duplicates()` runs at the top of every attempt and names the colliding pair, turning a mystery auth failure into a stated cause. (3) `repair_nat_duplicates()` reloads the worker of each pair — a **reload** is what actually moves a guest; the in-guest `networkctl renew` the 2026-07-29 row recommends does not, the server just re-offers the same lease to the same MAC (measured 40/40 no-move). Discriminator: distinct MACs holding one IP means the DHCP server, not the guests; read it with `vmrun getGuestIPAddress` AND from inside the guest, never from `vagrant ssh-config` (which reports the forwarded port and so inherits the confusion being diagnosed) |
| 2026-08-05 | `ip route del default` was the whole of "enforce isolation", and it does not hold: DHCP renewal makes systemd-networkd install the gateway again. Measured on the finished cluster — all six nodes had their default route back while the install log showed it removed on each. Provisioning itself did run isolated (that is when every leak surfaced), so the check looked like it worked; what it could not do is keep the delivered cluster offline, which means a later "still works offline?" test would be testing nothing | A networkd drop-in (`[DHCPv4] UseGateway=false / UseRoutes=false`) next to the file that manages the link, then delete the route. Resolve that file with `networkctl status <if>` — do NOT glob for `*<iface>*.network`: netplan names the rendered file after the netplan id (`10-netplan-main_if.network` for `enp2s0` on this box), so the glob matched nothing, created no drop-in, and reported success. Verified against a forced `networkctl renew`: no route returns, NAT address and host-only address both intact. Second lesson in the same fix — a removal that a daemon can undo is a pause, not a policy. **Two Kakao corrections (2026-08-06):** the fix landed after the Kakao cluster was built, so those six nodes had their routes back too (measured: `proto dhcp` default + internet 301) — a durable-isolation fix protects only clusters built after it, existing ones need it applied by hand. And on Kakao the drop-in must be `UseGateway=false` ONLY: the lease also delivers the metadata/NTP endpoints as classless routes (`169.254.169.254 via 172.17.0.4`), and `UseRoutes=false` discards those with the gateway, breaking cloud-init on the next boot. On the Vagrant NAT link the lease carries only the gateway, so dropping `UseRoutes` there never did anything — check `ip route \| grep "proto dhcp"` before deciding what a lease can be stripped of |
| 2026-08-06 | The static regression suite died with exit 1 and **zero FAIL lines** the moment cd64ff4 removed the last public repoURL: R34's counting pipeline ends in `grep -v \| sort \| wc`, and with nothing left to count `grep -v` emits nothing and exits 1, which pipefail turns into death mid-suite. The check crashed precisely at its own success case, and it could never have been caught earlier — the failure needs the watched problem to be *fixed* first. The known non-matching-grep rule, violated in a check written the same day the rule was sharpened | `\|\| true` on the pipeline, empty→0 guard after. Discriminator worth keeping: a check suite exiting non-zero with no FAIL output has **crashed**, not failed — read the last check that printed and look at the next one in the file. And when writing a check that counts occurrences of a problem, test it against the zero case before shipping: that is the case it exists to reach |
| 2026-08-05 | Two Helm **hook/Job** images were absent from the airgap bundle: `cert-manager-startupapicheck` (blocked cert-manager's post-install and failed 08-1 networking) and `kube-webhook-certgen` (kube-prometheus-stack's admission patch Job). Both are invisible to `01-generate-image-list.sh --live`, which enumerates what a *running* cluster uses — a hook Job exists only during install and is gone before any scan. The controller/webhook/cainjector images of the very same chart were all present, which is what makes the gap look impossible at first | Add hook images explicitly; longer term the generator needs `helm template --include-crds` over the bundled charts rather than a live scan, because no amount of scanning a healthy cluster will ever show them. Catching them cheaply: watch the live cluster for `ImagePullBackOff`/`ErrImagePull` during Phase 2 and top the mirror up as they appear — that found both in minutes, where reading charts would have been guesswork. Not every pull failure is a list gap: `kyverno/cleanup-controller` and `reports-controller` were in `images.txt` AND in the registry catalog, yet no node could pull them until re-pushed with skopeo; cause undetermined (neighbouring kyverno images stored the correct arm64, so it was not architecture) |
| 2026-08-05 | `05-nfs-quota-agent.sh` untarred the bundled chart and then failed with `Error: path "/tmp/nfs-quota-agent-chart" not found` — after the extraction had reported nothing wrong. The tarball's top-level directory is `nfs-quota-agent/`; the `-chart` suffix only ever matched the GitHub tarball the script used to download. Swapping the source (public URL → `chart` from the bundle) preserved the command but not the layout, and `tar` exits 0 either way | `tar xzf "$(chart X)" -C "${CHART_DIR}" --strip-components=1` so the destination is named by the script rather than by the archive. Discriminator: a helm "path not found" immediately after a clean extraction means the directory name, not a missing file — `tar tzf` the bundle and compare. General form: when a fetch is replaced by a bundle lookup, re-check what the artifact *unpacks into*, not just that the command succeeds; this is the same shape as the bin/manifests mount gap in the row below |
| 2026-08-05 | An offline install reached the CNI step and stopped at `ERROR: 'helm' is not in /home/vagrant/bin`. The binary was in the bundle the whole time — `lib-charts.sh` resolves three directories (`NARWHAL_CHART_DIR`, `NARWHAL_BIN_DIR`, `NARWHAL_MANIFEST_DIR`), the Kakao path stages all three, and the Vagrantfile mounted only `charts` and `apt`. The bundle-building work and the mounting work were done in different passes, so nothing compared the two lists | Mount `bin/` and `manifests/` alongside `charts/`; the three belong together and adding one without the others is what produced the gap. Verified by remounting a live node rather than by re-running the install: `-x` survives the HGFS mount and `/home/vagrant/bin/helm version` prints v4.2.1. Cheap general check before any rebuild — diff what the scripts reference (`chart`/`manifest`/`install_bin` call sites) against what the bundle actually contains; it caught nothing else here, which is the point |
| 2026-09-28 | The install had moved from downloads to bundle-only lookups, but the precondition check had not moved to the front. With only an amd64 bundle present, the Apple-silicon Vagrantfile mounted no `bin/`, `manifests/`, or `charts/`; six VMs joined, then about an hour later CNI failed because `helm` was absent. Separately, the arm64 chart build reported `Failed: 0` after saving 21 charts, while four `chart()` references (APISIX ingress controller, CloudNative-PG, NFS CSI, and Gitea) were missing; the older amd64 bundle hid the list drift | Run a bundle preflight before the first VM boots, using the Vagrantfile's architecture suffix and the same install-call scanner as chart generation. Make chart generation fail when a scanned install reference has no saved source/version. Discriminator: when downloads become bundle-only, check the host-architecture bundle before provisioning; a successful save count proves only that the script's current list downloaded successfully, not that the install's list is covered |
| 2026-08-05 | `kubeadm init` succeeded, then `kubeadm init phase upload-certs` died with `error: cannot use "0.0.0.0" as the bind address for the API Server`. The phase was invoked without `--config`, so kubeadm built a default InitConfiguration with an empty advertiseAddress and inferred one from the default route — which the airgap step had deliberately removed. Online it never failed *and never agreed*: the inference picked the NAT interface while `kubeadm init` had been given `MASTER1_IP`, and nothing reported the mismatch | Pass `--config=/tmp/kubeadm-config.yaml` to the phase so it reads the same config the cluster was initialized with. A/B on the live node: without it the error, with it a certificate key. General rule: any `kubeadm` subcommand run without `--config` re-derives its address from routing — `02-join-control-plane.sh` already appends `--apiserver-advertise-address` for exactly this reason. Related, same run: `kubernetesVersion: stable` made kubeadm fetch `dl.k8s.io/release/stable.txt`; it fails safe to the binary's own version, so offline it was only a warning, but the control-plane version was being decided by a network lookup — now pinned to `v${K8S_PATCH_VERSION}` |
| 2026-08-05 | With containerd mirrors configured and every other pull served offline, `00-kube-vip.sh` still died with `dial tcp 20.200.245.241:443: network is unreachable`. `/etc/containerd/certs.d` is read by containerd's **CRI plugin**, so kubelet and crictl inherit the mirror for free — but `ctr` is a direct client of the content store and resolves images itself, ignoring certs.d entirely. It was the one image pull in the provisioning path that does not go through CRI, and therefore the only one still reaching the internet | `sudo ctr image pull --hosts-dir /etc/containerd/certs.d <ref>`. Proven by A/B on the same isolated node: without the flag `network is unreachable`, with it `Completed pull ... elapsed: 0.7 s` from the mirror. Discriminator: mirror works for everything *except* one step → look for a non-CRI image client (`ctr`, `nerdctl`, kaniko, skopeo), not for a missing hosts.toml. regression-check R30 now fails any `ctr ... pull` without `--hosts-dir` |
| 2026-08-05 | The apt bundle carried only what `01-prerequisites.sh` installs, because that is the script the airgap work started from. dnsmasq (04-addons) and nfs-kernel-server/quota/quotatool (05-nfs-quota-agent) were absent, each one a `has no installation candidate` waiting 25 minutes into an offline run. The list was also duplicated inside 07 — once for the download, once for the dependency closure — so fixing one and not the other was the natural next mistake | One `AIRGAP_APT_PACKAGES` used by both, plus a coverage check in 07 that scans the provisioning scripts for what they actually `apt-get` and refuses to build a short bundle. The scanner is where the real care went: it must match installs guarded by `dpkg -s x \|\| ...` (not at line start), join backslash continuations, and **not** strip flags with `s/-[a-z]+//g` — that turns `nfs-common` into `nfs` and the check passes while the bundle is still wrong. Bundle went 123 → 140 .debs |
| 2026-08-05 | The first AIRGAP=1 clean install died on every node at the same line: `E: Failed to fetch file:/home/vagrant/apt/./Packages  File not found`. The index was not missing — `Packages.gz` was there, 0644, and root could read it. apt drops privileges to the `_apt` user before fetching, and `/home/vagrant` is `0750 vagrant:vagrant`, so `_apt` could not traverse into the mount. apt reports that as a missing **uncompressed** `Packages` (it silently gives up on `.gz` first), which points at bundle generation instead of at directory permissions | Mount the apt bundle at `/srv/airgap/apt` (0755 root:root all the way down), not under `/home/vagrant`. Vagrant's own copy at `/mnt/vagrant-mounts` is `0700` and fails the same way, so re-pointing at it is not an escape. Discriminator: `sudo -u _apt test -r <dir>/Packages.gz` — root-can/`_apt`-cannot is this bug, and `namei -m` names the offending directory. 01-prerequisites.sh now runs that check before writing the sources line, so the next occurrence says "not readable by the _apt user" instead of "File not found". Only `apt/` moved; `charts/` stays under `/home/vagrant` because helm runs as root and `lib-charts.sh` resolves it there |
| 2026-08-02 | `kubectl get nodes` returned `host-172-17-0-*` to a session that read them as the local Vagrant cluster's nodes. Nothing had gone wrong with the cluster: another session had run `set-config-kakao.sh` hours earlier, and both set-config scripts end with `kubectl config use-context`, which rewrites current-context in the **shared** `~/.kube/config` for every shell on the machine. Each script printed only its destination, so a move off another cluster was invisible unless you already knew where you had been — and the two contexts differ by one suffix. Sibling of the 2026-07-29 `set-config.sh` row below: same script, but that one exits silently while this one succeeds silently | Print both ends (`switched: <from> -> <to>`) and warn when the previous context pointed at a different API server. Discriminate on the **server**, never the context name: the first attempt tested whether the old name started with `CLUSTER_NAME`, and `narwhal-kakao` starts with `narwhal`, so the single move that matters read as a routine auth change — verified against the live kubeconfig, the prefix test stays silent exactly where the server test fires. For readers: `kubectl --context <name>` sidesteps the shared setting entirely, and is worth using in any script or doc that must not care what another session did |
| 2026-07-29 | "Cluster unreachable" had three unrelated causes stacked, and each looked like the others. (1) All six VMs were halted. (2) macOS Local Network Privacy was blocking the terminal app, so every unicast to `192.168.56.0/24` died while ARP still resolved and the internet still worked — `route get` pointed at the right bridge with the right MAC, and a `tcpdump` on the VM's `enp26s0` saw *zero* of four pings sent from the host. Own-interface + internet OK while *every* LAN target fails is macOS Local Network Privacy, not the cluster — enable the terminal app under System Settings > Privacy & Security > Local Network. For the duplicate lease, verify uniqueness with `vmrun getGuestIPAddress` per machine, not `vagrant ssh-config`. **Narwhal#223 (2026-09-28) added a distinct failure shape:** Phase 2 failed silently because `up.sh` fell through to exit 0 after five attempts, while `no route to host` / `EHOSTUNREACH` came from macOS Local Network Privacy on the terminal app (Orca), not the network — the host interface and `127.0.0.1` forwarded SSH port still worked. The live issue also reported `06-phase2-start.sh` returning rc=0 after `Critical script 07-cnpg.sh failed`; current source checked for this fix exits 1 in that critical-failure branch, so that historical return was not reproduced from the current script. **Superseded on 2026-08-05 in two ways** (see the row dated that day): the in-guest `networkctl` renew recommended here does NOT move a guest — the server re-offers the same lease to the same MAC — a `vagrant reload` does; and cause (3) turned out to be far more than a one-off, it is the long-unexplained per-run SSH failure |
| 2026-07-29 | `set-config.sh` printed its banner and exited silently against halted VMs — twice, and both times it read as success. `CONFIG=$(vagrant ssh master-1 -c "cat ~/.kube/config" 2>/dev/null)` swallowed the reason and `set -e` killed the script before `kubectl config use-context`, leaving the *previous* context active. It also reached master-1 over the NAT network, which proves nothing about whether kubectl can reach the API server | Never `2>/dev/null` a command whose failure ends the script. Fetch over the same network the resulting kubeconfig points at (host-only `MASTER_IP`), so a successful fetch proves the context is usable, and preflight both "is the VM running" and "does the API server answer from *this* machine" before writing any config |
| 2026-07-06 | Assumed `ghcr.io/dasomel/goharbor/*:latest` tracked Harbor v2.15.2 (the source release that prompted a chart bump) solely because `docker manifest inspect` showed multi-arch (amd64+arm64) support — that only proves the tag has ARM64 builds, it says nothing about which app version is behind it. | 2026-01-29 | Vagrant Cloud 401 error | Use `VAGRANT_CLOUD_TOKEN=$(hcp auth print-access-token)` when HCP token required |
| 2026-01-29 | charts.keycloak.org URL 404 | Use official Keycloak Operator (`keycloak-k8s-resources` GitHub) |
| 2026-01-29 | Bitnami image not found | Use official images or Operators (CloudNative-PG, etc.) due to Bitnami commercialization |
| 2026-01-29 | kubeadm init fails on vagrant provision re-run | Add logic to check if cluster already initialized, or run individual scripts directly |
| 2026-01-29 | Keycloak DB connection fails with FQDN | Use short service name in same namespace (`keycloak-db-rw` not `keycloak-db-rw.keycloak.svc.cluster.local`) |
| 2026-01-29 | Gitea Helm chart valkey FQDN issue | `gitea-init` secret script uses FQDN, needs patching. Set `valkey.enabled=true`, `valkey-cluster.enabled=false` in script |
| 2026-01-29 | Keycloak 2 replicas resource shortage | Use `instances: 1` in test environment, HA only for production |
| 2026-01-30 | Cluster DNS not accessible from API server | Expose OIDC issuer URL via NodePort (`http://NODE_IP:30080/realms/kubernetes`) |
| 2026-01-31 | OpenBao v2.4.4 no ARM64 image | No ARM64 at quay.io/openbao/openbao, use tag `2.2.0` (default registry) |
| 2026-01-31 | Velero bitnami/kubectl image not found | Bitnami banned, use `docker.io/alpine/k8s` (includes shell, ARM64 support) |
| 2026-01-31 | Pod eviction due to disk pressure | Add `node.kubernetes.io/disk-pressure:NoSchedule` to `tolerations` |
| 2026-01-31 | GitHub Actions get-changed-files doesn't support workflow_dispatch | Add `if: github.event_name != 'workflow_dispatch'` condition |
| 2026-01-31 | CNPG cluster replica WAL archiving failure | Delete problematic replica PVC and wait for auto-recreation, reduce instance count to stabilize |

Apply `--force-conflicts` |
| 2026-02-10 | Harbor ARM64 `exec format error` | Use `ghcr.io/dasomel/goharbor/*:latest`, official images are AMD64 only |
| 2026-02-10 | OAuth2 Proxy cookie_secret 34-byte error | `openssl rand -base64 32` produces 44 bytes, use `openssl rand -hex 16` for 32 bytes |
| 2026-02-10 | Velero CRD job kubectl missing `/bin/sh` | `registry.k8s.io/kubectl` is distroless, use `docker.io/alpine/k8s` |
| 2026-02-10 | VERSIONS.md out of sync with actual deployed versions | Must sync VERSIONS.md based on chart versions pinned in scripts |
| 2026-02-11 | CNPG DB password hardcoding mismatch | Must query actual password from CNPG Secret (`harbor-db-credentials`) |
| 2026-02-11 | Loki 6.52.0 bucketNames required | Must specify `loki.storage.bucketNames.{chunks,ruler,admin}` |
| 2026-02-11 | Traefik 39.0.0 HTTPS certificateRefs required | TLS certificate reference required for Gateway HTTPS listener |
| 2026-02-11 | Traefik Helm --set port type error | Use values file instead of `--set` (float64 vs int64) |
| 2026-02-11 | ArgoCD managed resource Helm reinstall conflict | Delete existing resources (SA, IngressClass, GatewayClass) before reinstall |
| 2026-02-28 | Keycloak HTTPRoute + Operator Ingress both active -> 502 | Keycloak Operator manages Ingress automatically, do not create HTTPRoute. HTTPRoute + Ingress coexisting on same Host causes Traefik WRR null backend selection |
| 2026-02-28 | Keycloak pod restart loses `istio.io/dataplane-mode: none` label | Must set permanently in Keycloak CR `spec.unsupported.podTemplate.metadata.labels`. Direct pod label addition is lost on restart |
| 2026-06-17 | `up.sh` Phase 2 call used `\|\| true` + final `echo`-only exit, so clean-install failures (SSH key race, mid-provision disconnect) silently exited 0 | Capture Phase 2 provision exit code; on non-zero, recover master-1 SSH and retry once; exit 1 on failure so the caller sees a real error |
| 2026-06-17 | `vagrant provision` over VMware SSH key race kills only the 06-phase2 orchestrator via SIGHUP; sub-scripts (08-1 onward) become orphans and Phase 2 stalls mid-way; yet master-1 is k8s-Ready so the main retry loop skips it | `up.sh` now checks `master1_ssh_ok` before Phase 2 and calls `recover_master1_ssh` (`vagrant reload master-1` + 40×15s poll) on failure; 3-master HA keeps the control plane quorate during the single-node reboot; phase2-platform is idempotent so the retry completes safely |
| 2026-06-17 | Cluster nodes' systemd-resolved leaked `harbor.local.narwhal.internal` to the NAT interface (public DNS) → resolved to a public AWS IP → image pull failed with `tls: unrecognized name` | Add `192.168.56.200 harbor.local.narwhal.internal` (APISIX LB VIP) to each node's `/etc/hosts` in `01-prerequisites.sh`, and enable containerd `config_path` + a `certs.d/harbor.local.narwhal.internal/hosts.toml` with `skip_verify = true` in `02-containerd.sh` (internal registry behind APISIX — no node CA trust needed) |
| 2026-06-30 | containerd 2.2.2 (Ubuntu 26.04) emits `config_path = ''` (single-quoted) under plugin `io.containerd.cri.v1.images`; the old grep/sed in `02-containerd.sh` matched neither the quote style nor the 2.x namespace → config_path stayed empty on workers → certs.d ignored → Harbor (private CA) image pull TLS fail → portal ImagePullBackOff (D3) | Replace grep/sed with a `python3 re.sub` that handles both quote styles (`''`/`""`) and both plugin namespaces (`cri.v1.images` / `grpc.v1.cri`); exits 1 loudly if no registry section. (2e49e5e) |

(273dcbc) |
| 2026-06-30 | Vagrantfile fired phase2-platform from a worker-3 `trigger.after :up`, but under `up.sh`'s per-VM resilient bring-up worker join order isn't guaranteed → trigger fired before all nodes stably Ready → nodes flapped (6→0→6) during Phase 2 → `08-1` kubectl ops failed (D7) | Remove the auto-trigger; make `scripts/up.sh` the sole Phase 2 driver (it polls all nodes Ready then runs phase2-platform once). | 2026-06-30 | VMware SSH key-replacement race SIGHUPs the Phase 2 orchestrator mid-run (sub-scripts orphan, Phase 2 stalls partway — e.g. monitoring/storage ns never created); `up.sh`'s single retry was insufficient when the race recurred (D11) | `up.sh` Phase 2 is now a bounded retry loop (`PHASE2_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3`): recover_master1_ssh before each attempt, run phase2-platform (idempotent — resumes where it stalled), gate on phase2_complete, exit 1 only after all attempts. (a426b73) |

| 2026-07-01 | A host reboot / `vagrant up` resume ran NO recovery (the Phase-2 `cilium_health_gate` only fires during provisioning), so a cold boot left a containerd stale-container wedge, Cilium/Istio `Unknown` ghosts, and platform CrashLoops — ingress dead until manual containerd restarts + pod cleanup | Two in-guest systemd oneshot units installed at provision, fired every boot: `narwhal-boot-heal.service` (all nodes — detect wedge, restart containerd+kubelet) + `narwhal-cluster-heal.service` (master-1 — restart not-Ready CNI, delete `Unknown` ghosts, kick CrashLoops, poll convergence). | 2026-07-01 | After reboot, static pods (etcd/apiserver/scheduler/controller-manager) showed `0/1 Running` with `<invalid>` restart ages: no working time sync (earlier "chrony not found") meant the kubelet started before chrony's `makestep` fixed the clock, so static-pod startTimes were recorded against a skewed clock → readiness never reconciled | Install chrony reliably; add `narwhal-clock-sync.service` (`chronyc waitsync`) + a `kubelet.service.d` drop-in so kubelet only starts once the clock is synced. To clear it on an already-skewed node: sync the clock then restart kubelet. (070149d) |
| 2026-07-01 | containerd 2.x `systemctl restart/stop containerd` leaves the **shim** alive; its child (the real container process) reparents to PID 1 and keeps holding ports/sockets/bolt-DB entries → `CreateContainerError` "name reserved" persists even after a containerd restart (bit deeper masters/openbao/etcd after reboot) | A plain containerd restart clears the common wedge (boot-heal), but a deep wedge needs: kill the reparented child PIDs, remove the CRI state dir `/var/lib/containerd/io.containerd.grpc.v1.cri/containers/`, AND `ctr -n k8s.io containers rm <id>` to drop the bolt-DB name reservation, then restart kubelet. |
| 2026-07-07 | Fresh clean install: ALL harbor pods CrashLoopBackOff with `exec /harbor/entrypoint.sh: exec format error` on arm64 nodes, even though `ghcr.io/dasomel/goharbor/*:latest` is a valid multi-arch image (the registry's arm64 variant is a genuine aarch64 binary, confirmed via `crane export`). Root cause: the `dasomel/ubuntu-26.04-xfs` Vagrant box bakes STALE amd64 `:latest` harbor layers into its containerd content store; a **mutable** tag is never re-fetched (a re-pull transfers only the ~16 KB manifest and reuses the baked amd64 layers — even `ctr pull --platform arm64` or pull-by-digest just fetch the manifest), so arm64 nodes execute amd64 binaries. Directly contradicts the older 2026-02-10 lesson ("use `:latest`") — for a baked box `:latest` is the trap. | **Pin harbor images to an IMMUTABLE version tag** in BOTH `08-5-registry.sh` (helm `--set`) and `harbor.yaml`: `:v2.15.1` for core/jobservice/registry-photon/registryctl/portal/nginx/redis-photon. A different tag has different layer digests absent from the stale bake → forces a real arm64 layer pull (verified `ctr run :v2.15.1 uname -m` → aarch64). **harbor-exporter EXCEPTION**: `ghcr.io/dasomel/goharbor/harbor-exporter` has NO clean `v2.15.x` multi-arch tag (only `:latest` + `:v2.15.0-build.NN`), so it can't follow v2.15.1; instead `metrics.enabled=false` (exporter Deployment never created, so the arch issue can't bite) and pin the unused override to `:v2.15.0-build.32`. Never trust `:latest` on a pre-baked box. (0e9a1fd) |
| 2026-07-08 | Falco 0.39.2 (chart 4.16.0) DaemonSet CrashLoopBackOff on every node: modern_ebpf probe fails `scap_init` ("An error occurred in an event source, forcing termination" → "Initialization issues during scap_init") on Ubuntu 26.04 kernel 7.0.0. NOT a config problem — BTF present (`/sys/kernel/btf/vmlinux`), pod privileged, driver already `modern_ebpf` (CO-RE). Falco 0.39.2's bundled libscap/libpman predates kernel 7.0. A live upgrade to 0.43.1 revealed it is a BREAKING migration, not a version bump: `--cri` CLI flag removed (`Option 'cri' does not exist`); container.* fields moved to a separate container plugin in Falco 0.41+, so the bundled rules fail to compile (`filter_check called with nonexistent field container.id`); falco.yaml/rules schema changed — it never reached scap_init, so even 0.43.x kernel-7.0 support is unconfirmed. | Disabled Falco in gitops (`falco.yaml` wrapped in `{{- if false }}` with rationale) to stop the crashloop; runtime coverage remains via trivy-operator (vuln scan), kyverno (policy), NetworkPolicy, and STRICT mTLS. A real fix is a dedicated Falco 0.43+ migration (args + falco.yaml + rules + container plugin via falcoctl) with `scap_init` verified on kernel 7.0, OR pin the box to a 6.x LTS kernel. **Follow-up 2026-10-28 (D-FALCO-045, row above):** migrated to 0.45.1 and re-enabled; the same `scap_init` failure reproduces for 0.39.2 on a kernel 7.0.14 VM and is gone with 0.45.1 there (Vagrant box itself not observed). |
| 2026-03-09 | 14-gitops-bootstrap.sh `GITEA_ADMIN_PASSWORD="gitea-admin"` hardcoded -> mismatch with actual generated password | Dynamically query with `kubectl get secret gitea-admin -n devtools -o jsonpath='{.data.admin-password}' \| base64 -d` |
| 2026-03-09 | oauth2-proxy-secrets missing `client-id` key -> `CreateContainerConfigError` | Add `--from-literal=client-id=oauth2-proxy` when creating secret in 11-3-keycloak-clients.sh |
| 2026-06-17 | 11-3이 apisix용 `platform-system/gitea-oidc-secret`(키 client_secret/session_secret)과 storage용 `velero-ui-oauth`(키 client_secret)을 생성하지 않아 apisix·velero-ui가 `CreateContainerConfigError` | 11-3 Group B에 gitea(platform-system) 추가 + storage/velero-ui-oauth 생성 추가로 해소 |
| 2026-03-23 → 08-04 | `set -o pipefail` kills a script from **two** directions, and knowing only the first is what caused the second. (a) A `grep` with no match exits 1 and takes the pipeline with it. (b) A **successful** downstream command that closes the pipe early — `awk '{print; exit}'`, `head -1` — makes the still-writing upstream take SIGPIPE, and pipefail reports that as failure: measured rc=141 (128+13) on `apt-cache madison kubelet \| awk '... exit'`. On 2026-08-04 that aborted 03-k8s-install.sh on all six nodes before its own error handling could print anything, so the log showed a banner and then silence, and up.sh spent all 6 Phase 1 attempts on nodes with no kubelet | For (a) `\|\| true`. For (b) **`head -1` is not the fix — it is the cause**, which the earlier version of this row and the CLAUDE.md rule both got wrong by prescribing it. Take the first match inside the filter without exiting (`awk '$3 ~ v && !seen {print $3; seen=1}'`), or terminate the whole substitution with `\|\| true`. Symptom to recognise: a script that dies with no message at all right after a pipeline — see the 2026-07-30 join row for the same signature |
| 2026-03-29 | `kubeadm join` without `--node-name` registers worker as `vagrant-ubuntu` (OS default hostname) instead of `narwhal-worker-X` | Always pass `--node-name $(hostname)` to `kubeadm join`. Even if `01-prerequisites.sh` sets hostname, kubeadm may use the OS default. Fix: `02-join-worker.sh` now includes `--node-name`. Recovery: `kubectl drain + delete node`, then `kubeadm reset -f && kubeadm join --node-name narwhal-worker-X` |
| 2026-04-06 | Keycloak StatefulSet 인데 `kubectl exec -n iam deploy/keycloak` 사용 | Keycloak Operator는 StatefulSet 생성. `kubectl exec -n iam keycloak-0 -c keycloak` 사용 |
| 2026-04-06 | `kcadm.sh` protocol mapper config 형식 오류 `{"config":{...}}` | 올바른 형식: `-s 'config={"key":"value"}'` (NOT `-s '{"config":{...}}'`) |
| 2026-04-06 | bash 함수 내 `echo` stdout 캡처 문제 → secret 변수에 로그 메시지 혼입 | 함수 내 모든 로그용 echo에 `>&2` 추가. `$(func)` 캡처 시 stdout만 반환됨 |
| 2026-04-06 | `KC_HOSTNAME_PORT` 은 Keycloak v1 옵션 (v26에서 무시됨) | Keycloak v2(v26+): `hostname.hostname` + `proxy.headers: xforwarded` 사용. 포트 제거는 X-Forwarded-Port 헤더로 처리 |
| 2026-04-06 | APISIX IC + ExternalName 서비스: `conflict headless service and backend resolve granularity` | ExternalName 서비스는 IC가 headless로 간주하여 `resolveGranularity: service`와 비호환. IC가 동기화 실패 시 APISIX admin API에 직접 패치로 우회 |
| 2026-04-06 | APISIX IC route 동기화 실패 시 admin API의 기존 route는 유지됨 | IC 실패 중에는 수동 admin API 패치가 overwrite되지 않음. `curl PATCH /apisix/admin/routes/{id}` 로 plugin 직접 추가 가능 |
| 2026-04-06 | Keycloak issuer URL에 `:9443` 포트 포함 → OpenBao OIDC discovery 실패 | APISIX `proxy-rewrite` plugin으로 `X-Forwarded-Port: 443` 설정 필요. IC 실패 시 admin API 직접 패치 |
| 2026-04-06 | API server manifest OIDC 플래그 중복 추가 (yq append 반복 실행 시) | 스크립트 실행 전 `grep -c 'oidc-issuer-url'` 으로 중복 확인. Python으로 중복 제거 후 재실행 |
| 2026-04-06 | Keycloak Operator-managed NetworkPolicy `keycloak-allow-hbone` blocks service port 8080 | Operator creates NetworkPolicy allowing only HBONE port 15008. When Keycloak is opted-out (`istio.io/dataplane-mode: none`), clients must connect via plain TCP 8080, not HBONE. Must create separate `keycloak-allow-plain` or add 8080 ingress rule to the NetworkPolicy |
| 2026-04-06 | StatefulSet pod stuck in old revision (ztunnel blocking probe) causes RollingUpdate deadlock | If pod is not Ready (startup probe fails), StatefulSet RollingUpdate cannot proceed even after CR/StatefulSet update. Old revision pod keeps running with wrong config. Fix: `kubectl delete pod keycloak-0 -n iam` to force recreation with new spec |
| 2026-04-06 | APISIX IC sets `nodes: []` for ExternalName service with `resolveGranularity: service` | IC cannot resolve ClusterIP for ExternalName (no ClusterIP). Remove `resolveGranularity: service` from ApisixRoute backend — IC then follows CNAME and sets FQDN as upstream node (same as ArgoCD pattern) |
| 2026-04-06 | Keycloak container image has no `curl` or `wget` (UBI9 minimal) | Cannot use `curl`-based exec probes. Use `bash /dev/tcp` or rely on Operator-managed httpGet probes. Operator ignores container probe overrides in `unsupported.podTemplate` |
| 2026-04-07 | APISIX container has no `curl` — `kubectl exec deploy/apisix -- curl` fails | Use master-1's curl directly: `curl http://$(kubectl get svc apisix-admin -n platform-system -o jsonpath='{.spec.clusterIP}'):9180/apisix/admin/...` |
| 2026-04-07 | nfs-quota-agent v0.2.1 HTML embeds Go raw-string-literal syntax `` ` + "`" + ` `` instead of actual backtick chars → JS SyntaxError → dashboard shows "Loading..." forever | Apply APISIX `serverless-post-function` body_filter with Lua `ngx.re.gsub` to replace all 22 occurrences at the gateway layer. **RESOLVED 2026-07-19 by upgrade to v0.3.0** (emits real backticks — verified 0 bug patterns / 17 real template literals through APISIX SSO); the gateway body_filter workaround was removed. |
| 2026-07-19 | Kyverno Enforce grandfather trap: nfs-quota-agent's running v0.2.1 pod (privileged + hostPID for XFS project quotas) was admitted BEFORE the `disallow-privileged-containers`/`disallow-host-namespaces` Enforce policies existed, so it kept running — but the v0.3.0 upgrade rollout hit `ReplicaFailure: FailedCreate` (webhook denied the NEW pod). A grandfathered privileged/hostPID/hostPort workload looks healthy until its next rollout/reschedule silently fails | When upgrading/rescheduling any privileged, hostPID/hostNetwork/hostIPC, or hostPort workload, FIRST confirm its namespace is in the relevant Kyverno policy `exclude` list — a running pod is NOT proof the policy allows it. Added `nfs-quota-agent` to both privileged + host-namespaces exclusions. Same class as the 2026-07-10 `=()` anchor lesson: Enforce policies bite at admission, i.e. on the NEXT create, not on already-running pods. |
| 2026-04-07 | APISIX IC stores `$secret://kubernetes/k8s-1/...` refs as plaintext in etcd when secret manager is not configured → openid-connect plugin sends literal string to Keycloak → `unauthorized_client` | Configure APISIX k8s-1 secret provider via admin API, OR patch the route directly via admin API with the plaintext secret (APISIX auto-encrypts on write) |
| 2026-04-07 | APISIX IC `ResourceSyncAborted` for ExternalName backends (no endpoints) — IC cannot sync route but doesn't re-create after admin API DELETE | Maintain these routes via admin API in provisioning scripts (11-3-keycloak-clients.sh). ApisixRoute YAML is documentation-only for these cases |
| 2026-04-07 | Grafana `grafana.ini` in Helm values generates `GF_AUTH_*` env vars; additional `kubectl patch deployment` adding same env vars → duplicate entries → ArgoCD `ComparisonError: duplicate entries for key` | Remove kubectl patch; manage all Grafana OAuth config solely via `prometheus-stack.yaml` grafana.ini. If live deployment has duplicates: Python dedup + `kubectl replace` |
| 2026-04-07 | `vault.hashicorp.com/agent-inject: "true"` annotation without `agent-inject-secret-*` path → OpenBao Agent Injector still injects init container → 403 `permission denied` on Kubernetes auth → pod stuck `Init:0/1` | Remove vault annotations when app uses `envFrom.secretRef` directly. Only add vault annotations when actual `agent-inject-secret-*` paths are configured |

### Cloud Deployment Mistakes (Kakao Cloud, 2026-07)

> Everything here surfaced only on a plain cloud image. The pre-baked Vagrant box provided
> each of these for free, so nothing in the repo had ever had to establish them.
> Architecture and the fixes in context: [`cloud-deployment.md`](../kakao/cloud-deployment.md).

| Date | Mistake | Fix |
|------|---------|-----|
| 2026-09-29 | narwhal#113's kubelet-authz work closed the *config-level* kubelet exposure (anonymous auth, webhook authn/authz, readOnlyPort) but left one finding open: the Kakao security group opened 10250 to `var.vpc_cidr` (the whole /16), not just the node subnet — the SAME pattern every other "(internal)" rule in `modules/security/main.tf` already uses (etcd, NFS, Cilium, metrics scrape ports, ...), so kubelet wasn't uniquely broad, it just happened to be the one named as a finding | Narrowed only the kubelet rule to a new `var.subnet_cidr` (wired from `module.network.subnet_cidr`, already output but unused by the security module) — the VPC's *other* subnet holds only the bastion, which never calls kubelet's API. R225 asserts it structurally (parses the 10250 rule's own block, not a file-wide grep, so a neighboring rule's `remote_ip_prefix` can't produce a false pass) and is mutation-verified. The other ~20 vpc_cidr-scoped rules are the same pattern and are explicitly NOT touched here — narrowing them is a separate, larger-blast-radius change outside what #113 asked for. |
| 2026-08-26 | `base` stage died on every node: `bash: line 2: /home/vagrant/base.log: Permission denied`. `provision-kakao.sh` connects as `SSH_USER=ubuntu` (`NODE_SSH_USER`), but `STAGE_DIR` defaults to `/home/vagrant` — correct for the *path*, since 30+ provisioning scripts hardcode the literal string `/home/vagrant/...` internally (source lines, `KUBECONFIG=`, `chown vagrant:vagrant`), independent of the `STAGE_DIR` variable; changing that default would desync the log/sentinel path from where the scripts actually live and run, a worse break than the one being fixed. The real bug: `run_scripts()`'s log redirect (`sudo flock ... sh -c '...' > ${STAGE_DIR}/${stage}.log`) opens the file with the OUTER `>`, on the SSH login shell running as the unprivileged `SSH_USER` — outside the `sudo` that elevates the actual stage scripts to root. `/home/vagrant` is writable by `ubuntu` only via the `vagrant` group membership `stage-kakao-nodes.sh`'s `ensure_vagrant_user()` grants, so any run where that step hasn't happened (or was clobbered by an earlier root-owned write) hits this | Move the redirect inside the sudo'd `sh -c` via `exec > ${STAGE_DIR}/${stage}.log 2>&1` as its first line, so root opens the log file — self-healing regardless of `/home/vagrant`'s ownership or whether `ensure_vagrant_user()` ran first. Discriminator: a script's *runtime identity* (which user's SSH session actually opens a file) is not the same as the *path contract* baked into its callees (`/home/vagrant` as a fixed, shared layout) — fixing a permission error by changing the path breaks the second thing while fixing the first. R92/R93 in `regression-check-kakao.sh` |
| 2026-08-27 | Independent code review (not a reproduced failure) of the fix above caught a second, distinct STAGE_DIR problem it didn't touch: `run_scripts()` still splices the env-overrideable `${STAGE_DIR}` unquoted into a root-elevated `sh -c '...'` string sent over ssh — `STAGE_DIR="/home/vagrant; rm -rf /; #"` would have that suffix parsed by the root shell, not treated as a path. The permission fix moved *who* opens the log file to root; it didn't change *what* gets built into root's command string, which is the separate question a reviewer has to ask about the same variable | Reject `STAGE_DIR` at startup unless it is an absolute path built only from `[A-Za-z0-9_/.-]` — fails closed before any of the half-dozen interpolation sites run, rather than quoting defensively at each one. Discriminator: fixing *who* runs a privileged command does not imply *what* it runs was already safe to build from unvalidated input — audit each concern separately even when they share one line of history |
| 2026-08-04 | "airgap" covered container images only. `03-save-helm-charts.sh` had bundled 24 chart tarballs since it was written and nothing read them; every script did `helm repo add <public url>`. Binaries (helm, cilium-cli, hubble, yq) and ten YAML manifests came from get.helm.sh and raw.githubusercontent.com at install time. The squid proxy made it all work, so the gap read as flakiness — two runs died on a helm fetch and a retry appeared to fix it | Resolve charts, binaries and manifests from the bundle with no internet fallback (`lib-charts.sh`), collected by `07-save-binaries.sh`. R28/R29 fail the build if a public fetch returns. A bundle nobody reads is indistinguishable from no bundle |
| 2026-08-05 | Ran `07-save-binaries.sh` for amd64 only, so the arm64 bundle the Vagrant path uses sat at bin=0 manifests=0 while amd64 had 4 and 10. Every local install would have died at the first `install_bin helm`, and no check caught it: they resolve whichever architecture matches the host, which here is the broken one, and only counted images | Verify both bundles, not the convenient one (R30). Two deployment targets means two of everything |
| 2026-08-02 | The helm 503 through squid came back on a clean run even though the IPv4-only DNS fix was in place and verifiably working — the same proxy answered 200 when probed by hand minutes later. `setup-bastion-proxy.sh` restarted squid **before** installing and starting dnsmasq, so squid came up pointing `dns_nameservers` at a resolver that did not exist yet, and cached the failure | Start dnsmasq first, squid second. When one daemon is configured to resolve through another on the same host, the order they are started in is part of the configuration — a fix that is present but sequenced wrong looks exactly like no fix at all |
| 2026-08-02 | The `base` stage reported `FAILED: common/02-containerd.sh` on worker-3, whose own log showed the full chain finishing cleanly. Killing the local driver does not kill the remote shell — it runs to completion — so resuming started the same stage on a node still working through the previous attempt, and the two fought over the apt lock. The loser's failure was reported against a node that was fine | Serialise per node: `sudo flock -w 7200 /var/lock/narwhal-stage.lock env … sh -c '…'`. flock takes its command as argv, so it adds no quoting layer. A driver that survives being killed needs mutual exclusion, not just idempotent steps |
| 2026-08-02 | Replacing the six instances, `master[2]` failed with `GetNetworkInterface: 404 Not Found` — but the instance had actually been created on the Kakao side. The provider died before writing it to state, leaving an orphan that held the pinned IP, so the retry failed with `Fixed IP address 172.17.0.12 is already in use`. `tofu import` does not rescue it either: import fills volume size and security group differently from the config, and the next plan then fails with `Attached volumes cannot be updated size` / `Changing the initial security group is not allowed` | Treat a failed `kakaocloud_instance` create as possibly-succeeded: list instances over the API, and if the name is there, `tofu state rm` (if imported) then DELETE it and re-apply. Import is not a usable recovery for this resource type |
| 2026-08-01 | Pinned mountd with `RPCMOUNTDOPTS` in `/etc/default/nfs-kernel-server` and it was silently ignored — that file is the sysv-era path, while the systemd units read `/etc/nfs.conf`. `rpcinfo -p` showed mountd on 49793/53677/… while lockd, pinned by sysctl, sat correctly on 4045. The v3 mount then timed out: the client asks rpcbind for mountd's port, gets one the security group drops, and waits | Write `/etc/nfs.conf.d/*.conf` instead, and **assert the result** — `01-nfs-server.sh` now fails loudly if `rpcinfo` does not report 20048. A config file that is read by nothing looks identical to one that works until something asks for the port |
| 2026-08-01 | The v3 switch was declared done when the StorageClass showed `nfsvers=3`, but the first PVC stayed Pending for 10 minutes and Phase 2 died on its very first script. The storage class was only the half that is easy to see; the RPC plumbing underneath it had never been exercised | For a protocol change, the passing test is a real mount and a real write from a real node — not the setting that requests it |
| 2026-09-28 | A least-privilege change to a shared export was verified statically only; the first live install showed that squashing root removes the path by which the CSI stack grants the workload uid write access. The provisioner creates PV directories as `nobody:nogroup 0755`, kubelet's `fsGroup` chown is denied by `root_squash`, and Grafana's root `initChownData` container independently fails chowning its NFS PVC with `Operation not permitted` | Keep `root_squash` and the export root at `0750`; disable the NFS driver's fsGroup policy, disable Grafana `initChownData`, and converge `0777` only on depth-2 PV directories from the NFS server. Discriminator: **root_squash breaks not only kubelet fsGroup chown but every chart init container that chowns as root**. When a non-root pod cannot write a fresh PVC, inspect the directory mode, CSIDriver policy, and chart init containers; `mountPermissions` changes the shared export root and is not a safe repair. Require a live non-root PVC write plus an unchanged root-mode check |
| 2026-09-28 | Existing NFS CSI installs kept live `CSIDriver.spec.fsGroupPolicy: File` after the chart began omitting that field; Helm cannot patch the immutable field away, so kubelet continued attempting chown through `root_squash` | Before the Helm upgrade, delete only `nfs.csi.k8s.io` when the live policy is exactly `File`, then verify the recreated object no longer has `File`. Preserve other policies. Discriminator: a clean-install chart value does not repair immutable state already stored in the API |
| 2026-08-01 | Re-running `init` on an already-initialized master died at `cp: overwrite '/home/vagrant/.kube/config'?`. Both init and join copy admin.conf with `cp -i`, which asks a question there is no tty to answer, so the script fails. It never showed up before because it only triggers on the second run — and both scripts have an explicit idempotency guard that skips kubeadm and falls straight through to exactly this line, so re-running is a designed path, not an accident | `cp -f`. An interactive flag in a script that runs over ssh with no tty is a latent failure waiting for the first retry; grep the tree for `-i` on cp/mv/rm when auditing re-runnability |
| 2026-08-01 | `03-cni-install.sh` died on `curl: (56) CONNECT tunnel failed, response 503` fetching helm, while a direct curl from the bastion to the same URL returned 200. `get.helm.sh` answers with an IPv6 address first and the VPC has no IPv6 route; curl falls back to IPv4, squid tries the address it was given and fails. Squid 6 removed `dns_v4_first`, so there is no proxy-side knob | Point squid at the local dnsmasq and set `filter-AAAA` there. Same root cause as the `Acquire::ForceIPv4` that 01-prerequisites.sh already sets for apt — when one client on a host needs IPv4 pinned, every client on it does |
| 2026-08-01 | Added a success marker so a dropped ssh could not be mistaken for failed work, and wrote it as a separate line after the command instead of `&&`-ing it — so it was touched even when the scripts had failed. The `init` stage then reported ok on a run whose log said `FAILED: cluster/03-cni-install.sh`, and the cluster went on to phase 2 with no CNI at all | A marker written unconditionally is worse than no marker: it converts a loud failure into a silent one. Also clear the previous attempt's marker before starting, or a retry inherits evidence from the run before it |
| 2026-08-01 | The `base` stage reported `172.17.0.22 FAILED` on a node that had in fact completed every script — its log ended with "Boot-Heal Install Done" and no FAILED line, and kubeadm, yq, jq, containerd and the pinned statd port were all in place. ssh returned non-zero after the work finished, and the driver read the transport's verdict as the work's verdict, so `mark_done` never ran and the whole stage was retried | Have the node record its own success: the remote shell touches `<stage>.ok` as its last act, and when ssh reports failure the driver reconnects and asks the node. A dropped connection after completion is otherwise indistinguishable from a failure — and over a bastion hop, dropped connections are routine |
| 2026-07-31 | NFSv4.1 deadlocked the whole cluster's storage, and it was mutual: the server sat in `nfsd4_destroy_session -> nfsd4_probe_callback_sync -> __flush_workqueue` waiting for the callback workqueue to drain, while the client sat in `nfs4_destroy_clientid -> rpc_wait_bit_killable` waiting for the server's reply — the work item the server was flushing was the RPC to that same client. The workqueue is shared across clients, so one unresponsive node froze NFS for all of them: 8 of 10 unhealthy pods were on that node and GitOps never bootstrapped | Move the storage class to `nfsvers=3`, which has no clientid, sessions or callback workqueue, so the cycle cannot form. Rebooting one side is not a fix — the survivor re-establishes the same session pair within minutes, so both ends have to be cleared together |
| 2026-07-31 | Rebooted both deadlock ends to clear it, then went to apply the v3 change — and the wedge re-armed before I got there, because the nodes came back and kubelet immediately remounted using the still-v4.1 storage class | Change the configuration BEFORE clearing the deadlock, not after. A reboot only buys the window between boot and the first remount |
| 2026-07-31 | `mountOptions` are copied into each PV's spec at provision time, so editing the StorageClass does nothing for volumes that already exist — all 15 PVs still carried `nfsvers=4.1`. A storage-protocol change is therefore not a config edit; it needs either a per-PV patch with everything scaled down, or fresh nodes | Validate a mount-option change on a build where no PV exists yet. When a setting is baked in at creation, "I changed the default" and "the running system uses it" are different claims |
